FIRST AMENDMENT TO
TRIPLE NET LEASE AGREEMENT
THIS FIRST AMENDMENT TO TRIPLE NET LEASE AGREEMENT dated
September 30, 2020 (hereinafter referred to as “the Agreement”) by and between Teton Lane,
LLC, (hereinafter referred to as “Owner”), and Wyoming Downs OTB 14, LLC, (hereinafter
referred to as “Operator”).
WHEREAS, for and in consideration of the provisions, representations and mutual covenants
and conditions contained herein and in the referenced Agreement, the parties desire to AMEND
and MODIFY the above referenced Agreement, to-wit:
I.
Page 1, Section
1 is amended to INCLUDE the language as follows:
The Owner and Operator agree that the Premises shall be used and occupied for commercial
purposes, specifically a bar and restaurant, allowing for the sale of alcoholic and/or malt
beverages on the leased premises.
2.
In all other respects the terms and conditions of the Agreement shall remain in full force
and effect.
The Owner and Operator hereby reaffirm all terms and conditions of the
Agreement, except as modified by this First Amendment.
IN WIThESS WHEROF, the parties have;xecuted this FIRST AMENDMENT TO TRIPLE
NET LEASE AGREEMENT on this 21
day of__________ 2022.

WITNESSETH, THAT FOR AND IN CONSIDERATION of the covenants and agreements
herein contained to be observed, kept and performed by the aforementioned respective parties
hereto, the Lessor does hereby lease, let and demise unto the Lessee and the Lessee does hereby
lease and hire for the Lessor the premises located and situated at 1345 Dell Range Blvd,
Cheyenne, Wyoming 82009 (the “Premises’) in the
County of Laramie County.
The legal
description of the Premises is as follows:
A portion of Lot 1, Block 2, Meadowbrooke Park, a subdivision situated in the
North Half of Section 29, Township 14 North, Range 66 West of the 6th P.M., City
of Cheyenne, Laramie County, more particularly described as follows:

1.
USE OF PREMISES
The Premises shall be used only as or for the purpose of: An entertainment venue, and all uses
customarily associated with such purpose, including reasonable administrative space and storage.
2.
LEASE TERM
A. The term of this Lease shall begin on October 1st, 2020 and will end on December
31St 2025 at 11:59 PM (the “Initial Term”). This Lease may also be extended by the parties (an
“Extension”) and renewed (a “Renewal Term”), as described below. Collectively, the Initial
Term, and any Extension and Renewal Term entered into, shall be described in this Lease as the
“Term”.

B. In the event that the Lessee does not wish to renew the lease, the Lessee shall provide
the Lessor with ninety (90) days advance written notice of intent to vacate, and Lessee may
vacate at the end of the month in which the 90th day falls. Prior to vacating the Premises, the
Lessee shall make sure that the Premises are clean and free and clear of any dirt, trash, waste
and/or debris, with the exception of normal wear and tear. The Lessor shall have the right to
perform a walk through prior to the Lessee vacating to ensure the Premises comply with the
aforementioned requirements.
3.
RENT/LEASE PAYMENT
A. The following base rate rents are established for the first base period of 63 months

B. The Lessee agrees to and shall pay each monthly rental payment to the Lessor at P0
Box 1142, Cheyenne, Wyoming 82003, or through ACH transfer or other third party rent
collecting system by the tenth (10th) of each month.
C. Rent shall be payable in advance of the 1st calendar day of each month. Any payments
received after the 10th day of each month shall be deemed late and delinquent. Should the Lessor
not receive payment by the tenth day of the month in which the payment is due, the Lessee shall
pay a late charge of $100 per day, which shall be considered additional rent and due with the next
rental payment. Notwithstanding anything to the contrary in this Lease, Lessee’s failure to make
rental payments shall only be deemed a “material default” of Lessee’s obligations under this Lease
after Lessee has failed to make rental payments, when due, for three (3) consecutive months.
D. In the event a check for rent or lease payment is returned for non-sufficient funds
(NSF), the Lessee shall pay a return check fee in the amount of $100.00, and the Lessor shall
reserve the right to only accept further payments made in certified funds (e.g. certified check or
money order).
4.
RENEWAL TERMS
If the Lessee is not in default under the terms and conditions of this Agreement, the Lessee shall
have the option to renew this Lease for two (2) Renewal Term of five (5) years. If the Lessee
chooses to exercise this renewal option, the Lessee shall provide to the Lessor written notice of
Lessee’s intention to renew at least 90 days prior to the expiration of the initial Term. The

Renewal Term shall continue upon the same terms and conditions in this Agreement, with 3%
annual increase in rent/sq foot.
5.
HOLDING OVER
Failure of the Lessee to surrender the Premises at expiration of the Term constitutes a
holding over which shall be construed as a “tenancy-at-will” or a month to month lease at
the rate of $22,000.00 per month, until such time as the Lessee and Lessor enter into a
renewal or extension of the Lease, or Lessee provides notice of intent to vacate.
6.
SECURITY and RENT DEPOSIT
A. Concurrently with the execution of this Lease, the Lessee shall deliver to the
Lessor the amount of $20,000.00 as security for the performance of the Lessee of every
covenant and conditions of this Lease (the “Security Deposit”). Said Security Deposit may be
co-mingled with other funds of the Lessor and shall bear no interest. In addition to the security
deposit, the Lessee shall deliver first month ($17,274.92) and last months rent ($19,438) prior to
October 1st, 2020. Tenant requires the approval of the Wyoming Parimutuel Commission no
later than November 15, 2020. If the Commission does not approve the location, tenant to
forfeit first and last month rent deposit and seller to return the security deposit to tenant.
B. In the event that the Lessee defaults with respect to any covenant or condition of this
Lease, including, but not limited to the payment of rent, the Lessor reserves the option and right
to apply some or all of the paid Security Deposit to the payment of any sum in default or any
sum which the Lessor may be required to spend by reason of Lessee’s damage or default. If any
portion of the Security Deposit is so applied, the Lessee, upon demand by the Lessor, shall
deposit cash with the Lessor in an amount sufficient to restore the Security Deposit to its
original amount.
C. Lessor shall return the full amount of Lessee’s Security Deposit, without interest, upon
termination of the Lease so long as Lessee has complied with all the covenants and conditions
of this Lease.
7.
POSSESSION
A. The Lessee shall take possession of the Premises on October 1st, 2020, unless
otherwise agreed by the parties. The Lessor shall use due diligence to ensure Lessee is provided
possession of the Premises at the beginning of the Term of this Lease Agreement. The first
month’s rent shall be prorated for the period of any delay in providing or turning over
possession of the Premises to the Lessee; however, the length of the Term shall not be extended
as a result of any such delay. The Lessee shall bring no claim against the Lessor for any delay in
obtaining possession.
B. In the event that the Lessee fails to take possession of the Premises within 30 days of
October 1st, 2020, through no fault of Lessor, then the Lessor retains the right to terminate this
Lease.

C. In the event Lessee is unable to take possession of the Premises within thirty (30) days
of October 1St, 2020, as a result of Lessor’s actions (or those of Lessor’s owners, employees,
contractors or agents) Lessee may terminate this Lease immediately, without penalty or further
obligation to Lessor.
8.
INSURANCE ON PREMISES
The Lessor shall obtain and pay for general property, fire and extended coverage casualty
insurance for the building and other improvements on the Premises, and shall include Lessee as
an additional insured on such policies to the extent of Lessee’s leasehold interest.
9.
LESSEE INDEMNITY & LIABILITY INSURANCE
The Lessee shall at all times indemnify, defend and hold the Lessor harmless from all loss,
liability, costs, damage and expenses that may occur or be claimed with respect to any person or
persons, property on or about the Premises or to the Premises resulting from any act done or
omission by or through the Lessee, the Lessee’s agents, employees, staff, invitees or any person
on the Premises by reason of the Lessee’s use or occupancy or resulting from the Lessee’s non-
use or possession of the Premises and any and all loss, cost, liability or expense resulting
therefrom. Lessee shall maintain at all times during the Term comprehensive general liability
insurance with an insurance company that is licensed to do business in the state in which the
Premises are located and is satisfactory to Lessor, properly protecting and indemnifying Lessor
with single limit coverage of not less than $1 ,000,000.00 for injury or $1,000,000.00 for death
of persons and $500,000.00 for property damage. Lessee shall carry automobile and liability
insurance on its business and employees and in an amount of no less than $2,000,000 for bodily
injury, death, or property damage arising out of the ownership, maintenance, or use of motor
vehicle for the purpose of Lessee’s business and in a minimum amount of $500,000 for each
occurrence. All such policies shall be of primary coverage and non-contributory on the part of
the Lessor. During the Term Lessee shall furnish the Lessor with a certificate or certificates of
insurance, in a form acceptable to the Lessor, covering such insurance so maintained by the
Lessee and naming the Lessor and Lessor’s mortgagees, if any, as additional insureds.
10.
TAXES
Lessee shall pay, during the Term of this Lease, the real estate taxes and special taxes and
assessments (collectively, the “Taxes”) attributable to the Premises and accruing during such
term. Lessee shall pay to the Lessor said Taxes on annual basis within 60 (sixty) days of
receiving an invoice from the Lessor. Taxes for any fractional calendar year during the Term
hereof shall be prorated. In the event the Lessee does not make any Tax payment required
hereunder, Lessee shall be in default of this Lease.
Lessee shall be responsible for and shall pay before delinquency all municipal, county, or state
taxes assessed during the term of this Lease against any leasehold interest or personal property
of any kind owned by or placed in, upon, or about the Premises by the Lessee.

11.
OPERA TING EXPENSES
A.
It is the intention of the parties, and they hereby agree, that this shall be a triple
net Lease, and the Lessor shall have no obligation to provide any services, perform any acts
or pay any expenses, charges, obligations or costs of any kind whatsoever with respect to the
Premises, and Lessee will be responsible for exterior building maintenance, fire
suppression system maintenance, HVAC maintenance, roof repair and maintenance
and exterior lighting.
Lessee hereby agrees to pay one hundred percent (100%) of any and
all Operating Expenses as hereafter defined for the entire Term of the Lease and any thereof
in accordance with specific provisions hereinafter set forth. The term “Operating Expenses”
shall include all costs to Lessee of operating and maintaining the Premises and related
parking areas, and shall include, without limitation, real estate and personal property taxes
and assessments, management fee, heating, electricity, water, waste disposal, sewage,
operating materials and supplies, service agreements and charges, lawn care and landscaping,
snow removal, repairs, cleaning and custodial, security, liability insurance, the cost of
contesting the validity or applicability of any governmental acts which may affect
operating expenses, and all other direct operating costs of operating and maintaining the
Premises and related parking areas, including restriping and repaving if needed.
12.
ASSIGNMENT AND SUBLETTING
A.
The Lessee shall not assign, transfer or encumber this Lease and shall not
sublease the Premises or any part thereof or allow any other person to be in possession thereof
without prior written consent of the Lessor, in each and every instance. Said consent shall not
be unreasonably withheld by the Lessor. For the purpose of this provision, any transfer of a
majority or controlling interest in Lessee (whether in one or more related or unrelated
transactions), whether by transfer of stock, consolidation, merger, transfer of a partnership
interest or transfer of any or all of Lessee’s assets or otherwise, or by operation of law, shall be
deemed an assignment of this lease. Notwithstanding any permitted assignment or subletting,
Lessee shall at all times remain directly, primarily and fully responsible and liable for the
payment of the rent herein specified and for compliance with all of its other obligations under
the terms and provisions of this Lease.
B.
Lessor agrees that Lessee will have first right of refusal in the event Lessor
decides to sell, transfer, assign or convey its interest in the Premises during the Term, excepting
mortgages existing on the Premises as of the Effective Date. The first right of refusal offer and
responses shall be conducted in writing allowing at least 30 (thirty) days for consideration.
C.
Lessee has the option to purchase Property after three ( 3 ) years from date of
occupancy for a price determined to be at the then fair market value supported by a State of
Wyoming Licensed Appraiser and sale price agreed to by Lessee and Lessor.
13.
CONDITION OF PREMISES

14.
LESSOR’S RIGHT OF ENTRY
The Lessor or Lessor’s agent shall have the right of entry at reasonable hours to inspect or show
the Premises to prospective Lender or Lien Holders and purchasers, and to perform or provide
anything that the Lessor may be required to perform or provide hereunder, or which the Lessor
may deem necessary for the good or benefit of the Premises or any building of which they are a
part. As of and during the last ninety (90) days of this Lease, the Lessor shall have the right to
post and/or display a “For Rent” or ‘For Sale” sign on the Premises, unless Lessee has provided
notice of Lessee’s intent to enter into a Renewal Term of this Lease, as described above in
‘Renewal Terms.”
15.
EXCLUSION OF LESSEE
During the Term, Lessor may not intentionally prevent the Lessee from entering the Premises
except by judicial process unless the exclusion results from: (i) bona fide repairs, construction,
or an emergency, where such exclusion is necessary to Lessor’s interest in the Premises; or (ii)
removing the contents of Premises abandoned by Lessee, after Lessee has vacated the Premises.
Lessor or Lessor’s agent must then place a written notice on Lessee’s front door stating the name
and address or telephone number of company or the individual from whom the key may be
obtained. The new key is required to be provided only during Lessee’s regular business hours.
16.
SIGNS AND ADVERTISEMENTS
The Lessee shall not place upon nor permit to be placed upon any part of the Premises, any
signs, billboards or advertisements whatsoever, or paint the exterior walls of the building
without the advance prior written consent of the Lessor. The Lessor shall have the right to
remove any sign(s) which have not been approved in order to maintain the Premises or to make
any repairs or alterations thereto. All permitted signage placement and/or removal shall be at the
Lessee’s sole cost and expense. The parties agree that Lessee may place reasonable signage on
the Premises relevant to its intended use, and that such signage is deemed approved by Lessor.
17.
FORCE MAJEURE
A. In the event that the Lessor or Lessee is unable to reasonably perform its obligations

under this Agreement as a result of a natural disaster, war, terrorist activities, strike, lockout,
labor issues, civil commotion, and act of God, or any other event beyond the control of the
Lessor or Lessee, with the exception for non- availability of funds, the party shall not be in
breach of this Agreement if the party diligently perfonns the obligations after the end of the
force majeure event. The non-performing party shall give written notice to the other party as
soon as reasonably practicable in the event of non-performance due to a force majeure event.
B. In the event, during the Term, the Premises shall be destroyed or so damaged by fire or
other casualty as to become uninhabitable or unusable, then in such event, at the option of the
Lessor, this Lease shall terminate from the date of such damage and/or destruction. The Lessor
shall exercise this option to terminate this Lease by delivering written notice to the Lessee
within 30 days after the occurrence of such damage and/or destruction. Upon such notice, the
Lessee shall immediately surrender said Premises and all interest therein to the Lessor, and the
Lessee shall pay rent only to such time that damages and/or destruction occurred. In the event
that the Lessor does not elect to terminate this Lease, this Lease shall therefore continue in full
force and effect, but Lessee’s obligation to pay rent for that portion of the Premises rendered
unusable by fire or other casualty shall be abated during such period, and the Lessor shall
expeditiously make any and all necessary repairs to the Premises as needed, placing the same in
as good condition as it was prior to the occurrence of damage or destruction. Lessee’s
obligation to make rent payments as to the unusable portion shall resume once such repairs are
complete and the Premises may be reasonably used for Lessee’s full intended purpose. If the
unusable portion of the Premises is so great that the remainder of the Premises cannot
reasonably be used for Lessee’s full intended purpose for more than thirty (30) days, Lessee
may, at its option, terminate this Lease upon ninety (90) days prior written notice, with no
further penalty or obligation to Lessor.
18.
PERSONAL PROPERTY
The Lessor shall not be liable for any loss or damage to any merchandise inventory, goods,
fixtures, improvements or personal property of the Lessee in or about said Premises.
19.
ALTERATIONS
Any and all alterations, additions and/or improvements, except trade fixtures, installed at the
expense of the Lessee shall become the property of the Lessor and shall remain upon and shall
be surrendered with the Premises as a part thereof on the termination of this Lease. Such
alterations, additions, and improvements may only be made with the prior written consent and
approval of the Lessor, which shall not be unreasonably withheld. If consent is granted by the
Lessor for the making of improvements, alterations or additions to the Premises, such
improvements, alterations or additions shall not commence until such time as the Lessee has
furnished to the Lessor a copy of all plans and a certificate of insurance showing coverage in an
amount satisfactory to the Lessor protecting the Lessor from liability for injury to any person
and damage to any personal property, on or off the Premises, in connection with the making of
such improvements, alterations or additions. No cooling tower, equipment, or structure of any
kind shall be placed on the roof or elsewhere on the Premises by the Lessee without prior
written permission of the Lessor. If such permission is granted, such work or installation shall

equipment or structure if directed to do so by the Lessor. However, the Lessor shall retain the
option to permit the Lessee to remove their alterations or improvements prior to the expiration
of this Lease and return the Premises to its original condition. Lessee shall promptly repair, at
its expense, any damages resulting from such removal. Any damage caused by the installation
of trade fixtures shall be repaired at the Lessee’s expense prior to the expiration of the lease
term. All alterations, improvements, additions and repairs made by the Lessee shall be made in
good and workmanlike manner.
20.
UTILITIES & SER VICES
The Lessee shall fumish and pay for all of the following and any other utilities deemed
necessary by the Lessee at the Premises: Gas, electricity, HVAC, water, internet, and
telephone, and shall make all necessary arrangements for all such utilities to be separately
metered and billed to Lessee.
21.
INTERRUPTION OF UTILITIES
Lessor or Lessor’s agent may not interrupt or cause the interruption of utility services paid
directly to the utility company by the Lessee unless interruption results from bona fide repairs,
construction, or an emergency. If any utility services furnished by the Lessor are interrupted and
continue to be interrupted despite the good faith efforts of Lessor to remedy the same, Lessor
shall not be liable in any respect for damages to the person or property of Lessee or Lessee’s
employees, agents, or guests and same shall not be construed as grounds for constructive
eviction or abatement of rent. Lessor shall use reasonable diligence to repair and remedy such
interruption promptly.
22.
LEGAL REQUIREMENTS
The Lessee shall comply with all laws, orders, ordinances and other public requirements now
and hereafter affecting the Premises or the use thereof and the Lessee shall indemnify, defend
and hold harmless the Lessor from any expense or damage resulting from Lessee’s failure to do
so.
23.
REPAIRS AND MAINTENANCE
In the event that any repairs required to be made by the Lessee hereunder are not made within 90

days after written notice delivered to the Lessee by the Lessor, the Lessor shall reserve the right
and option to make or have said repairs made without liability to the Lessor for any loss or damage
which may result by reason of such repairs, and that Lessee shall pay to the Lessor, upon demand
as additional rent hereunder, the reasonable cost of such repairs. At the termination of this Lease,
Lessee shall deliver the Premises in good order and condition, normal wear and tear excepted.
Normal wear and tear meaning the deterioration which results from normal use and not as an act
of carelessness, neglect, accident or abuse.
24.
EMINENT DOMAIN
In the event that the Premises are taken under the power of eminent domain or a conveyance in
lieu thereof by any authority having the right of condemnation, or if a portion thereof is taken so
that the Premises are unsuitable, in the Lessee’s reasonable opinion, for Lessee’s use, then the
term of this lease shall terminate as of the date that title shall vest in the acquiring authority, and
the rent and other charges shall be adjusted as of the date of such taking. In such case, the
Lessor shall be entitled to the proceeds of the condemnation award made to the Lessor. Nothing
herein shall be construed to prevent the Lessee from separately pursuing a claim against the
condemning authority for its independent loss or damages to the extent available. The Lessee
shall have no claim against the Lessor for the value of the unexpired term of this Lease, but may
seek compensation from the condemning authority for such value. Should any part of the
Premises be taken in the exercise of eminent domain or a conveyance in lieu thereof or in
connection therewith, but not such as to render the Premises unsuitable for the operation of
Lessee’s business, this Lease shall continue on the same terms and conditions except that the
description of the Premises or the real estate taken by right of eminent domain or conveyance in
lieu thereof or in connection therewith shall be modified to reflect such taking. In the event this
Lease does not terminate by reason of such taking, the condemnation proceeds from the
Premises will first be used to restore the Premises to a position of occupancy by the Lessee. The
balance of such condemnation proceeds from the Premises, if any, shall belong to the Lessor.
25.
WAIVER OF SUBROGATION
As part of the consideration for this Lease, each of the parties hereby releases the other party
from all liability for damage due to any act or neglect of the other party occasioned to the
property owned by said parties which is or might be incident to or the result of fire or other
casualty against loss for which either of the parties is now carrying or hereafter carry insurance;
provided however, that the releases herein contained shall not apply to any loss or damage
occasioned by intentional acts of either of the parties, and the parties further covenant that any
insurance they obtain on their respective properties shall contain an appropriate provision
whereby the insurance company, or companies, consent to the mutual release of liability contain
in this paragraph.
26.
DEFAULT & REMEDIES
A.
RE-ENTRY: If Lessee commits a material default of this Lease, Lessor shall
provide Lessee with written notice of the facts or condition alleged to constitute default, and

Lessee shall have thirty (30) days (or such longer time as is agreed by the parties) to cure the
default. If Lessee cures the material default to Lessor’s reasonable satisfaction within such
period, the notice shall be deemed withdrawn and the default cured. If the material default is not
cured within such period, the Lessor shall re-enter the Premises without declaring the Term
ended and relet the whole or any part therefor for the account of Lessee on such terms and
conditions and at such rent as Lessor may reasonably deem proper, collecting such rent and
applying it to the amount due from Lessee hereunder as mitigation to any damages suffered by
Lessor as a result of such default. Lessor may recover from Lessee the expense of such reletting
(including expense of alteration and special inducements to Lessee) and any other damage or
expense so sustained by Lessor, or any such item or items, from the difference between the
proceeds of such reletting and the amount of rental payments Lessee is obligated to pay
hereunder. As soon as the difference between the rental rate on re-letting and the rental
payments required by this Lease equals the amount of any due but unpaid rent by Lessee as of
the date of re-letting, the Term shall be ended and this Lease terminated, and Lessee shall
thereafter have no further obligation to Lessor hereunder.
B.
LESSEE DEFAULT AND REMOVAL OF ABANDONED PROPERTY
In the event that the Lessee abandons the Premises or otherwise materially defaults in the
performance of any obligations or covenants herein (except the failure to pay rental payments
required when due), the Lessor may enforce the performance of the Lease in any manner
provided by law. This Lease may be terminated at the Lessor’s discretion if such
abandonment or default continues for a period of sixty (60) days after the Lessor notifies the
Lessee of such abandonment or default and of Lessor’s intention to declare this Lease
terminated. Such notice shall be sent by the Lessor to the Lessee at the Lessee’s last known
address by certified mail. If Lessee has not completely removed or cured the default within
the 60 day period, this Lease shall terminate. Thereafter, Lessor or its agents shall have the
right without further notice or demand to enter the Premises and remove all property
without being deemed guilty of trespass and without waiving any other remedies for
arrears of rent or breach of covenant. Upon abandonment or material default by the
Lessee, the remaining unpaid portion of any rent shall become due and payable. For the sole
purpose of this section, Lessee is presumed to have abandoned the Premises if goods,
equipment, or other property, in an amount substantial enough to indicate a probable intent to
abandon the Premises, is being or has been removed from the Premises and the removal is
not within the normal course of Lessee’s business. Lessor shall have the right to store any
property of Lessee that remains on the abandoned Premises and, in addition to Lessor’s other
rights, may dispose of the stored property if the Lessee does not claim the property within
fourteen (14) days after the date that the property is stored, provided Lessor delivers notice
by certified mail to Lessee.
C.
DAMAGES
Notwithstanding anything in this Lease to the contrary, Lessor shall make commercially
reasonable efforts to mitigate any damages it may suffer as a result of any default by Lessee,
including making reasonable efforts to re-let the Premises, and apply the proceeds of re-letting
to such damages. Lessor shall only be entitled to recover damages from Lessee as a result of

D.
LESSOR’S DEFAULT
In addition to any other right or remedy Lessee may have herein or at law or equity,
Lessee shall have the right, in the event of any material default of this Lease by Lessor, to
terminate this Lease upon sixty (60) days written notice to Lessor. The notice shall state the
events alleged to constitute material default, and Lessor may attempt to cure the alleged
material default during the 60 day notice period (if capable of cure). If the material default is
cured to Lessee’s reasonable satisfaction during such period, Lessee’s notice shall be deemed
withdrawn. If the default is not cured within such period, this Lease shall be immediately
terminated and Lessee shall have no further obligation to Lessor hereunder.
E.
RIGHT TO CURE DEFAULT
Either party, at any time after the other party commits a default, can cure the default at the
defaulting party’s cost. If the curing party at a time, by reason of the other party’s default, pays
any sum or does any act that requires the payment of any sum, the sum paid by the curing party
shall be due immediately from the defaulting party to the curing party at the time the sum is
paid. If the defaulting party is the Lessee, such amounts shall be paid by Lessee to Lessor as
additional rent.
27.
WAIVER
Unless otherwise stated herein, the rights and remedies of the Lessor and Lessee under this
Lease, as well as those provided by law, shall be cumulative, and none shall be exclusive of any
other rights or remedies. A waiver by the Lessor or Lessee of any breach or default of the other
party shall not be deemed or construed to be a continuing waiver of such breach or default nor
as a waiver of or permission, expressed or implied, for any subsequent breach or default. It is
agreed that the acceptance by the Lessor of any installment of rent subsequent to the date the
same should have been paid shall not alter the covenant and obligation of Lessee to pay
subsequent installments of rent promptly upon the due date. Receipt by Lessor of partial
payment after Lessee’s default shall not be construed to be or constitute a cure of any such
default. No receipt of money by Lessor before or after the termination of this Lease shall, on its
own, reinstate, continue or extend the Term.
28.
TOXIC OR HAZARDOUS MATERIALS
Lessee shall not store, use or dispose of any toxic or hazardous materials in, on or about the
Premises without the prior written consent of Lessor. Lessee, at its sole cost, shall comply with
all laws relating to Tenant’s storage, use and disposal of hazardous or toxic materials. Lessee
shall be solely responsible for and shall defend, indemnify and hold Lessor, its agents and
employees, harmless from and against all claims, costs and liabilities, including attorney’s fees
and costs, arising out of or in connection with the Lessee’s storage, use or disposal of any toxic

survive the termination of this Lease.
29.
GO VERNING LA WS
This Agreement shall be construed under and in accordance with the laws of the State of
Wyoming, without giving effect to its “conflict of laws” jurisprudence. Exclusive venue for
any action at law or in equity brought by any party to enforce or construe this Lease shall be in
the courts in and for Laramie County, Wyoming, and each party hereby irrevocably consents to
such court(s) exercise of personal jurisdiction over it.
30.
COMPLIANCE WITH LA WS AND REGULATIONS
Each party shall, at its own expense, comply with all laws, orders, codes and requirements
of all government entities with reference to the use and occupancy of the Premises. Each party
and its agents, employees, and invitees shall fully comply with any rules and regulations
governing the use of the buildings or other improvements to the Premises as required by the
Lessor and agreed to by Lessee.
31.
NOTICES
Any notice hereunder shall be sufficient if sent by certified mail, addressed to the Lessee at the
Premises, and to the Lessor where rent is payable.
32.
SUBORDINATION OF LEASE TO MORTGA GES
This Lease shall be subject and subordinate at all times to the lien of mortgages existing on the
Premises as of the Effective Date. With regard to any pledge or mortgage executed by the
Lessor, Lessor shall use its best efforts to provide to the Lessee a non-disturbance agreement
from any mortgagee or other lien holder of Lessor’s interest in the Premises. Such non-
disturbance agreement shall be in form and content reasonably acceptable to Lessee and
Lessor’s mortgagee or other lien holder, together with a representation that the Lessor is not in
default of any of the terms of any such mortgage or security agreement as of the date thereof.
The Lessee will execute and deliver such further reasonable instruments subordinating this
Lease to the lien of any such existing mortgages as may be desired by the mortgagee. Lessor
shall not default on any loan or other obligation secured by a mortgage on the Premises during
the Term, and shall provide Lessee with at least ninety (90) days prior notice of any
circumstances reasonably likely to lead to an event of default by Lessor on any such loan.
33.
SUCCESSORS
The provisions, covenants and conditions of this Lease shall bind and inure to the benefit of the
legal representatives, heirs, successors and assigns of each of the parties hereto, except that no

34.
QUIET POSSESSION
Lessor agrees, so long as Lessee materially complies with all of the terms, covenants and
conditions herein contained on the Lessee’s part to be kept and performed, Lessee shall and may
peaceably and quietly have, hold and enjoy the Premises for the Term and any extension or

quiet enjoyment shall binding upon the Lessor, its heirs, successors or assigns, but only during
such party’s ownership of the Premises. Lessor and Lessee further covenant and represent that
each has full right, title, power and authority to make, execute and deliver this Lease.
35.
BANKRUPTCY
Neither this Lease nor any interest therein nor any estate hereby created shall pass to any trustee
or receiver in bankruptcy or to any other receiver or assignee for the benefit of creditors by
operation of law or otherwise during the Term or any renewal thereof.
36.
PRIOR A GREEMENTS SUPERSEDED
This agreement constitutes the sole and only agreement of the parties to this lease and
supersedes any prior understandings, whether written or oral agreement, between the parties
respecting the subject matter of this lease.
37.
AMENDMENT
No amendment, modification, or alteration of the terms hereof shall be binding unless it is in
writing, dated subsequent to the date hereof and duly executed by all parties to this agreement.
38.
ADDITONAL INSTRUMENTS
The parties hereto will execute any and all additional document or instruments that may be
necessary or convenient to carry out the intent and purposes of the parties to this agreement.
39.
ENTIRE A GREEMENT
This Lease contains the entire agreement between the parties and no modification of this
Lease shall be binding upon the parties unless evidenced by an agreement in writing and
signed by the Lessor and Lessee after the date hereof. If there be more than one Lessee
name herein, the provisions of this Lease shall be applicable to and binding upon such
Lessees, jointly and severally.
40.
SHORT FORM MEMORANDUM OF LEASE
The parties hereby agree that, at Lessee’s request, each of them shall execute a short-form
memorandum of lease, evidencing Lessee’s leasehold interest in the Premises, and option to

January 27, 2022
RE: Letter of Introduction
Dear Mayor Collins and esteemed City Council Members of Cheyenne,
Wyoming Downs is a privately held LLC offering Historic Pari-Mutuel wagering and live
Simulcast Pari-Mutuel wagering at 17 locations (OTBs) across Wyoming. We currently have
locations in Evanston, Green River, Rock Springs, Laramie, Cheyenne, Casper, Gillette, and
Sheridan. Employing more than 100 Wyoming residents we are licensed by and in good
standing with the seven cities and counties in which we operate plus the Wyoming Pari-Mutuel
Commission and Wyoming Liquor Division, Dept. of Revenue. On an annual basis we
contribute over four million dollars to state, city and county taxes and over a million dollars to
the state’s Breeders Award Fund. We also own and operate Wyoming’s largest and only
privately owned live horse racing facility located in Evanston, Wyoming. We are a member in
good standing with Cheyenne’s Chamber of Commerce.
All of our employees are required to pass background checks and drug tests prior to employment,
complete TIPS (alcohol seller and server training), as well as successfully obtaining licensure
from the Wyoming Gaming Commission.
Eric Nelson
Managing Partner
Wyoming Downs
Rick Cook
Chief Operating Officer
Wyoming Downs

WYOMING DOWNS
CITY OF CHEYENNE
Who We Are
•
We are Wyoming Downs, LLC.
•
Wyoming Downs operates the only privately owned racetrack in Wyoming.
•
In 2021 we were approved and conducted 18 days of live racing in Evanston; we
have been approved for 20 race days in 2022.
•
Wyoming Downs is the largest contributor to the Wyoming Breeder Award Fund.
•
We currently operate 17 off-track betting (OTB) locations statewide.
•
Wyoming Downs is authorized by the State of Wyoming to conduct horse racing
events to include racing, simulcasting, historic racing, roping and a variety of
equestrian events.
•
WPC is funded by the sole efforts and fees of the live track operators.
•
In 2021 Wyoming Downs funded approximately $2,985,000.00 and any excess
funding goes into the state general funds positively impacting both the city and
county of Cheyenne.
•
We have maintained licensing from the Wyoming Pari-Mutuel Commission and
have a long history of conducting pari-mutuel business in Wyoming.
•
Wyoming Downs has always conducted business in accordance with the rules and
regulations of the Wyoming Pari-Mutuel Commission.
Business Plan
•
Our existing location is 1345 Dell Range Blvd., Cheyenne, WY 82009.
•
Wyoming Downs’ leased space currently hosts off-track wagering, pari-mutuel
simulcasting and approximately 134 historic horse racing terminals.
•
We are looking to apply and obtain a liquor license in order to serve liquor and
malt beverages to our patrons.
•
Liquor sales would allow Wyoming Downs to be competitive.
Benefits to Cheyenne
•
A big part of the success that Wyoming Downs, LLC has achieved is due to the
partnership(s) we have and are developing with Cheyenne and like cities and
counties throughout the state of Wyoming.
Page lof2

WYOMING OOWNS
•
It is projected that adding a liquor license will increase tax revenue to the City of
Cheyenne and County of Laramie by an estimated 25% and provide additional
jobs to the City of Cheyenne.
•
In 2022 we estimate an additional $65,000 in taxes will be paid to the City of
Cheyenne bringing the estimated total taxes to the city up to $282,000.
•

o
Wyoming Breeder Award Fund: $2,388,000

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2022-04-25_TotalExtraction_62275300.pdf
Wyoming
Association of
Municipalities
Building Strong Communities
TO:
All Mayors
FROM:
Earla Checchi, Finance Manager
SUBJECT:
Voting Delegates for the 2022 WAM Summer Convention
DATE:
February 16, 2022
We are requesting that your municipality’s governing body appoint its Official Voting Delegate and
alternate to WAM’s Summer Convention Business Meeting, held Thursday, June 9, 2022, in Laramie.
Items that your Voting Delegate will be voting on may include By-law changes, Resolutions,
Membership Dues. Any individual member of the association is entitled to speak during the June
business meeting. However, when a vote is taken on any action the official voting delegate, or the
alternate, is the only one allowed to vote for the city or town. Any elected or appointed official/staff
may be designated by the city/town as its official voting delegate.
Please complete the attached form and email, mail, or fax it to WAM by Friday, May 27, 2022. We
appreciate your cooperation and prompt action on this matter to ensure that each municipality is
represented by a person who has been duly authorized by your governing body to take an active role
in the business meeting.
If you find your official delegate is not able to attend the conference at the last moment, you may re
appoint someone else. For this change to be accepted we do need the change in writing. You may
send/fax it to the WAM office by Friday, May 27 or your voting delegate may bring the written
change/authorization to the convention and submit it to the WAM registration desk by Wednesday,
June 8 before 12:00pm. After that time, changes will not be accepted.
Please contact us with any questions.
Ensure YOUR community has a VOICE and a VOTE at the June business meeting!

WYOMING ASSOCIATION OF
MUNICIPALITIES
2022 WAM SUMMER CONVENTION
OFFICIAL VOTING DELEGATE FORM
The following person has been selected as the Official Voting Delegate for the 2022 WAM Summer
Convention Business Meeting in Laramie, Thursday, June 9, 2022.
City/Town:
Name:
Title:
Alternate Delegate will be:
______________________________________________________
Title:
-
Date Approved by the City/Town Council:
______________________________________
Attest:
(City/Town Clerk)
PLEASE EMAIL, MAIL OR FAX TO WAM NO LATER THAN May 27, 2022.
315 West 27 Street, Cheyenne, WY 82001
Phone (307) 632-0398, Fax (307) 632-1942 or
Email to Earla Checchi at: checchi@wyomuni.org

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2022-04-25_TotalExtraction_47138590.pdf
      
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
April 25, 2022 
 
 
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers and via electronic conference meeting.  Present were: MAYOR 
– Patrick Collins, COUNCIL MEMBERS – Dr. Michelle Aldrich, Bryan Cook, Ken Esquibel, 
Richard Johnson, Pete Laybourn, Scott Roybal, Tom Segrave and Jeff White. Absent: Dr. Mark 
Rinne.  Also present: Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk.  The pledge of allegiance was recited. 
 
 
Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to 
be routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no 
separate discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support 
by two other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered 
in its normal sequence on the agenda.  Mr. Segrave moved to approve, seconded by Mr. White.  
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.  
 
[CA] 
Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on April 11, 2022.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
      
Mayor Collins opened a public hearing to hear public comment for the City of Cheyenne’s 
Annual Action Plan Fiscal Year 2022 prepared by the Cheyenne Housing and Community 
Development Office.  Deanne Widauf, Housing and Community Development, provided a brief 
overview of the history and purpose of the Community Development Block Grant program, noting 
the proposed plan is consistent with the 5-year Comprehensive Consolidated Plan completed in 
2020, and stated the anticipated allocation funding amount is $485,401.00 and $123,504.00 in 
recaptured funding.  Ms. Widauf reviewed information on the grant application process; public 
notifications and opportunities for input; role of the Advisory Council in reviewing and 
recommending projects to be funded and explained the projects that are recommended for funding.  
Dr. Aldrich recognized the attendance of Housing and Community Development Advisory 
Council Chair Wendy Soto and expressed appreciation for those who work with and are a part of 
the council.  There being no further comments or input and purpose of the hearing having been 
met, Mayor Collins closed the public hearing. 
 
 
Mayor Collins opened a public hearing to receive comments and input for an amendment 
to the Hitching Post Urban Renewal Plan and Project as required by Wyoming Statute Pursuant to 
Wyoming Statute §15-9-109.  Mark Christensen, Planning and Development Department, 
provided information on the amendment which updates tax increment funding (TIF) and includes 
drainage and detention facilities.  He advised the original plan was approved in late 2021 and 
provided information on what is allowed under the plan.  He further noted the amendment is largely 
due to a proposed hotel in the area.  There being no further comments or input and purpose of the 
hearing having been met, Mayor Collins closed the public hearing. 
 

 
Mayor Collins opened a public hearing to receive comments and input for a transfer of 
ownership of retail liquor license application filed with the City Clerk’s Office for 307 Horse 
Racing, Inc. d/b/a The Derby Club - Cheyenne, 1510 E. Pershing, Cheyenne, WY (license 
currently held by Stults Management, LLC d/b/a The Office Bar & Eatery, 1600 E. Pershing 
Blvd.). Kyle Ridgeway, 307 First, explained his businesses is the parent company of 307 Horse 
Racing, Inc. and responsible for redevelopment of the Pershing Plaza and provided information on 
the background of the retail liquor license. Mr. Ridgway explained 307 Horse Racing, Inc. has met 
Planning and Development and zoning requirements for the proposed location.  He further 
provided information on the business which will include a bar and off track betting machines on 
the first floor and the next phase will include different types of businesses in the upper level of the 
building which may include an arcade, axe throwing, etc.  Mr. Ridgeway noted they have been 
involved in multiple communities in the state.  There being no further comments or input and 
purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor Collins closed the public hearing and referred the 
application to the Finance Committee. 
 
 
Mayor Collins opened a public hearing to receive comments and input for a bar and grill 
license application filed with the City Clerk’s Office for Stults Management LLC d/b/a The Office 
Bar & Eatery, 1600 E. Pershing Blvd. #100, Cheyenne, WY.  Kyle Ridgeway, speaking on behalf 
of The Office Bar & Eatery, advised the business is transferring ownership of their retail liquor 
license and will instead utilize a bar and grill license for the establishment.  There being no further 
comments or input and purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor Collins closed the public 
hearing and referred the application to the Finance Committee. 
 
ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Creating a new Section 9.01.010, Definitions of Title 9, 
Public Peace and Welfare, of the Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Reporting for Public 
Services Committee, Mr. Cook moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. 
Roybal. Mr. Cook moved to amend by substitute dated April 11, 2022, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  
Mayor Collins advised the substitute removes the definition of harassment.  Motion to amend by 
substitute carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present with the exception 
of Dr. Aldrich voting “no”.   Upon inquiry, Mayor Collins explained the purpose of the substitute 
is to remove the definition of harassment because it evoked stalking laws per state statute.  Main 
motion as amended carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present with the 
exception of Dr. Aldrich and Mr. Segrave voting “no”. (#4427) 
 
ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Repealing Ordinance No. 3465, and reestablishing and 
redefining ward boundaries within the City of Cheyenne.  Jordan Evans, Coordinator of GIS 
Cooperative, provided information on the boundary changes noting the city and county have 
worked together on the boundary changes.  Boyd Wiggam, advocated for the Council to consider 
expanding the number of wards, referenced a Wyoming Tribune-Eagle editorial which also 
recommended expanding wards and noted results of a poll where a majority of people that 
participated agreed that wards should be expanded.  Mr. Wiggam requested that if there is adequate 
interest for expansion of wards, to not advance the ordinance and let a process through the county 
take place.  Mr. Cook moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Mr. 
Cook moved to amend by substitute dated April 18, 2022, seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Mayor Collins 
explained the substitute includes actual survey language that was previously not included and that 
the map didn’t change.  Comment was made that constituents are interested in expanding wards, 

however due to timing constraints, it is not conducive at this time.  Motion to amend by substitute 
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Evans 
provided current population amounts for each ward using census data and noted the counts do not 
include anyone living in the Sweetgrass development.  Main motion as amended carried.  Voting 
“yes” – all members of the governing body present.  (#4428) 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Amending the City of Cheyenne’s Solid Waste Fee 
Schedule.  Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. White moved to approve on third and final 
reading, seconded by Mr. Johnson.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing 
body present with the exception of Mr. Cook voting “no”. (#4429) 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Increasing the compensation for the Mayor of the City 
of Cheyenne, commencing January 1, 2025.  Mr. White moved to approve on third and final 
reading, seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing 
body present with the exception of Dr. Aldrich voting “no” and Mayor Collins who abstained. 
(#4430)  
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Increasing the compensation for members of the City 
Council of the City of Cheyenne and providing an effective date.  Mr. White moved to approve on 
third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Motion carried. Voting “yes” – all members of 
the governing body present with the exception of Dr. Aldrich, Mr. Cook and Mr. White voting 
“no”. (#4431)    
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from AR Agricultural Residential to Harmony Valley 
Planned Unit Development (Ordinance #4274) for land located north of West College Drive and 
east of York Avenue.  Mr. Cook moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.   
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Amending Section 10.52.030, Parking Not to Obstruct 
Traffic, of Chapter 10.52, Stopping, Standing and Parking Generally, of Title 10, Vehicles and 
Traffic, of the Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Mr. Cook moved to approve on second 
reading, seconded by Mr. Johnson.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing 
body present.   
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Amending Section 9.12.080, Electric Fences Prohibited, 
of Chapter 9.12, Offenses Against Property, of Title 9, Public Peace and Welfare, of the Code of 
the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Mr. Cook moved to approve on second reading, seconded by 
Mr. Johnson.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.   
 
ORDINANCE – 1st READING – Amending Section 10.52.230 Downtown 
Construction/Maintenance Worker Parking in an Established Timed Parking Zone, of Chapter 
10.52, Stopping, Standing and Parking Generally, of Title 10, Vehicles and Traffic, of the Code of 
the City of Cheyenne – referred to Finance Committee. (SPONSOR – MR. WHITE) 
   

 
RESOLUTION – Amending the City of Cheyenne and Board of Public Utilities 
Construction Specifications and Standard Drawings, 2014 Edition.  Mr. Cook moved to adopt, 
seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Mr. Cook moved to amend by substitute dated April 9, 2022, seconded 
by Mr. Segrave.  Frank Strong, Board of Public Utilities, explained the substitute is to update the 
water meter pit detail drawing which is included in what is required for installation.  Motion to 
amend by substitute carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.  Main 
motion as amended carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.  (#6227) 
 
[CA] 
RESOLUTION – Adopting the City of Cheyenne Annual Action Plan 2022, prepared by 
the Cheyenne Housing and Community Development Office – referred to Finance Committee. 
(SPONSOR – DR. ALDRICH) 
 
[CA] 
RESOLUTION – Adopting a City of Cheyenne Healthcare Plan, a City of Cheyenne 
Health Benefit Trust, and appointing fiduciaries for the Plan and Trust – referred to Finance 
Committee. (SPONSOR – MR. WHITE)    
    
[CA] 
RESOLUTION – Accepting a U.S. Department of Homeland Security Federal Emergency 
Management Agency Fiscal Year 2020 Building Resilient Infrastructure and Communities Grant 
– referred to Finance Committee. (SPONSOR – MR. WHITE) 
 
[CA] 
RESOLUTION – Declaring that the Municipalities of Laramie County have agreed that 
the current State Statutes regarding the designation of liquor licenses issued by the governing 
bodies of said municipalities are stifling economic growth, and are requesting the State Legislative 
committees and State Legislature review, consider, and adopt modifications to state statutes 
regarding said licenses – referred to Finance Committee. (SPONSORS – DR. ALDRICH, MR. 
COOK, MR. ESQUIBEL, MR. JOHNSON, DR. RINNE, MR. ROYBAL AND MR. WHITE) 
 
[CA] 
RESOLUTION – Authorizing submission of an application to the Wyoming Department 
of Transportation for a Wyoming Rural Public Transportation Program Grant to provide the local 
match for operating funds for the City of Cheyenne for its Transit Program, as authorized by 49 
U.S.C § 5311 (5311 WYDOT Grant) – referred to Finance Committee. (SPONSOR – MR. 
WHITE) 
 
[CA] 
RESOLUTION – Approving an amendment to the Hitching Post Urban Renewal Plan and 
the Hitching Post Urban Renewal Project Pursuant to Wyoming Statute §15-9-110 – referred to 
Finance Committee. (SPONSOR – MR. SEGRAVE)    
 
[CA] 
RESOLUTION – Pursuant to Wyoming Statute §15-9-107 finding and declaring parts of 
the 15th Street Corridor in the City of Cheyenne as blight and authorizing the redevelopment 
thereof – referred to Public Services Committee.  (SPONSOR – MR. SEGRAVE) 
 
[CA] 
RESOLUTION – Re-appropriating the Fiscal Year 2022 Budget of the City of Cheyenne 
according to the provisions of W.S. §§ 16-4-112 & 16-4-113 – referred to Finance Committee. 
(SPONSOR – MR. WHITE) 
 

[CA] 
RESOLUTION – Supporting the City of Cheyenne’s application for a United States 
Department of Commerce, Economic Development Administration American Rescue Plan Act 
Travel, Tourism, and Outdoor Recreation Grant for the 15th Street Railcar Renovation Project – 
referred to Finance Committee. (SPONSOR – MR. WHITE) 
 
 
Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Martin/Martin 
Wyoming, Inc. for the Belvoir Ranch Pedestrian Bridge – Civil, Bridge Design, and 
Bidding/Construction Services (Belvoir Ranch Recreation Fund).  Mr. White moved to approve in 
an amount not to exceed $185,700.00, seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Comments and concerns were 
expressed on the following topics: concern of expending money for bridge design when there are 
many unknowns including bridge location in conjunction with existing railroad tracks, Belvoir 
Ranch access, inquiry as to how the agreement came to be, actual bridge expenditures, concerns 
relating to agreement and budget allocation, agreement with railroad and reference to an email 
from Domenic Bravo, Visit Cheyenne, that provides information on a grant for different elements 
at the Belvoir Ranch.   Upon inquiry, Mayor Collins advised that in order to access the wind 
turbines and the trailhead, more roads will need to be built.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all 
members of the governing body present with the exception of Dr. Aldrich voting “no”. (#7507) 
 
[CA] 
Memorandum of Understanding between Laramie County Weed & Pest Control District 
and the City of Cheyenne Weed & Pest Department for funding of the goat grazing on the Dry 
Creek and Crow Creek drainage projects – referred to Finance Committee. (Revenue to the City)  
 
[CA] 
Memorandum of Understanding between the City of Cheyenne and Laramie County 
School District Number One to provide Civic Center usage to Laramie County School District 
Number One Music Department – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] 
Consideration of second Development Agreement between the City of Cheyenne, Swagger 
Construction, LLC, and Banner Capital Bank for Tax Increment Financing for the Hitching Post 
Urban Renewal Plan & Project (TIF Funding) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] 
Contract Modification #1 to Contract #7465 between the City of Cheyenne and Plan One / 
Architects for the Emergency Response Facility Design and Construction Administration Services 
Project adding civil infrastructure design (Specific Purpose Sales Tax) – referred to Finance 
Committee. 
 
[CA] 
Contract Modification #1 to Bid #E-7-21 for Ice Control Product for the City of Cheyenne 
Street & Alley Division (2019-2022 1% Sales Tax) – referred to Finance Committee.                 
 
[CA] 
Contract Modification #1 to Contract #7379 between the City of Cheyenne and Lance & 
Hall LLP for renewal of public defense services (General Fund) – referred to Finance Committee. 
          
[CA] 
Consideration of Bid #E-15-22 for furnishing two (2) new small articulated loaders and 
one (1) new heavy duty vehicle for the City of Cheyenne, Community Recreation & Events 
Department (2019-2022 1% Sales Tax Funding).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (accepted 
bid from Bobcat of the Rockies, Cheyenne, in an amount not to exceed $186,380.00).

One available retail liquor license application filed with the City Clerk’s Office by Ace’s 
Range LLC d/b/a Ace’s Range LLC, 1505 Pioneer Ave., Big D Oil Co. d/b/a Big D Oil #56, 4100 
Whitney Rd., Billy Jacks - Wyoming LLC d/b/a Billy Jack’s Pizza Pub, 4020 Airport Parkway 
West, Suite 1, Capitol Cuisine LLC d/b/a Ike’s Bar & Grill, 122 W. 6th St., Ful-O-Pep d/b/a The 
Old Cheyenne Elevator, 800 W. 21st St., Get Bent, LLC d/b/a Get Bent, LLC, 707 W. Lincolnway,  
Peaches’ Package Liquor & Bar LLC d/b/a Peaches’ Package Liquor & Bar LLC, Anderson 
Corner, 3rd Filing, Lot 2, Block 1, Vitamin Cottage Natural Food Markets, Inc. d/b/a Natural 
Grocers, 1851 Dell Range Blvd., Westby Edge LLC d/b/a Westby Edge LLC, 714 W. 20th St. and 
Wyoming Downs OTB 14 LLC d/b/a Wyoming Downs OTB 14, 1345 Dell Range Blvd., 
Cheyenne.  Mayor announced Billy Jack’s Pizza Pub has withdrawn their application and has 
submitted an application for a bar & grill license and announced public comments will be limited 
to 3 minutes.  Seth Stefanik, co-owner of Los Conejos and Micro Popup Concepts, provided 
background information on the Los Conejos food truck explaining it was a very successful startup 
business during Covid. He provided information on the “ghost kitchen” concept noting it is a 
professional food preparation and cooking facility for delivery-only meals and doesn’t include a 
storefront or indoor seating. The concept also includes QR codes posted around town that can be 
accessed for ghost kitchen information including how to place orders and information on deliveries 
and pickups.  Darrin Westby, Westby Edge LLC, expressed appreciation to the governing body 
for consideration of their application and also their consideration for the resolution addressing 
stifling state statutes involving liquor licenses in hopes that legislative changes are made.  Mr. 
Westby advised he appreciates focus on blighted areas through town and acknowledged council 

members were placed in an unfortunate situation, but applicants were also put in a difficult position 
as they were competing against friends.  April Brimmer Kunz, Aces Range LLC, echoed Mr. 
Westby’s concerns with antiquated liquor laws and expressed appreciation to the governing body 
for their efforts to initiate change.  Ms. Brimmer Kunz advised they will proceed with opening 
their business and continue to work to bring logic to the liquor licensing process and that noted 
she fully intends to engage as a lobbyist for not only Aces Range LLC but also for the city and 
state.  She noted that she had learned there are 55 available licenses in the county and expressed 
support for Get Bent, LLC.  Mayor Collins explained the 55 county licenses can’t be used within 
5 miles of city limits so they would have to be utilized in rural areas.  Chad Willett, Get Bent, 
LLC, advised they have applied for a pre-application meeting that is scheduled for this week with 
the Planning and Development Department for conditional use.  Mr. Willett noted they will 
continue to stand with colleagues for reform of antiquated liquor license requirements moving 
forward and applauded members of the governing body for their resolution addressing stifling state 
statutes involving liquor licenses.  Jen Seitz, The Old Cheyenne Elevator, spoke in support of all 
projects, voiced concern regarding reverting the license based on a timeline as it may be difficult 
to achieve.  She spoke about the Metropolitan and how they opened a craft liquor store while the 
remainder of the business was being completed and noted 7 months is a very tight timeframe to 
achieve a certificate of occupancy due to supply chain and other factors.  Ms. Seitz referenced how 
long it took the Municipal Building to reopen to the public due to supply chain issues and advised 
state statutes outline a timeframe in which businesses have to become operational and suggested 
there not be any additional restrictions added to the license.  Mr. Roybal moved to approve Get 
Bent, LLC d/b/a Get Bent, LLC, 707 W. Lincolnway, with the following condition: “if the license 
does not receive a full certificate of occupancy by December 31, 2022, the license reverts in whole 
to the City of Cheyenne”, seconded by Mr. Esquibel.  Upon inquiry, City Attorney Stefanie Boster 
explained a full certificate of occupancy would require that the Fire Marshall and Building 
Department inspect and approve, all pertinent city codes are met including the planning process 
with a site plan.  The following comments were made related to the application for Get Bent, LLC: 
applicant does not meet the guidelines of restoring a blighted building, concerns of the business 
fully utilizing the license in regards to a full service restaurant, and very limited space for package 
store, dining, bar etc., timeline provided by Planning and Development Director Charles Bloom 
indicates it will be a challenge for the business to meet the conditions timeline and opinion that 
Westby Edge is closer to meeting all requirements.  Upon inquiry, Mayor Collins advised the 
governing body would have the ability to extend the deadline.  City Clerk Kris Jones further 
explained the timeline extension would go back before the governing body for approval. Ms. 
Boster advised the governing body is currently awarding the liquor license and the license won’t 
be issued until a full certificate of occupancy is received, noting the council has full authority to 
bring the license back before the governing body for consideration of a timeline extension.  
Discussion continued and included: support for the Get Bent, LLC concept downtown, available 
liquor license options for other applicants, difficulty meeting timeframe requirement, request to 
exercise grace while working with the licensee regarding the timeframe requirement, and request 
that any anticipated delays be shared with the council.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members 
of the governing body with the exception of Mayor Collins and Mr. Segrave voting “no”.   
 
Upon inquiry, Robin Lockman, City Treasurer provided information on a voucher payable 
to Christa McDonald for volunteer hours for the Foster Grandparent program noting federal funds 
are received for the program and distributed to the program volunteers by the city.  Dr. Aldrich 

provided further information advising it is a pass-through program and volunteers receive minimal 
compensation.  Vicki Nemecek, upon inquiry, provided information on a voucher payable to 
Cheryl Y. Tottenhoff Living Trust noting the Street and Alley rental is across the street from their 
facility and is used to store sweepers.  No further comments were made on the Voucher Report as 
presented. 
 
No comments were made on the Revenue Report as presented. 
 
Appointment of Robert C. Jarosh as City Hearing Officer for the City of Cheyenne.  Dr. 
Aldrich moved to approve, seconded by Mr. White.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members 
of the governing body with the exception of Mayor Collins who did not vote (mayoral 
appointment). 
 
Appointment of Brian Eicholtz to the International Fire Code Board of Appeals.  Mr. 
Segrave moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members 
of the governing body with the exception of Mayor Collins who did not vote (mayoral 
appointment). 
 
Reappointments of Kyle Wall and Lane Pilbin to the International Fire Code Board of 
Appeals.  Mr. Segrave moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” 
– all members of the governing body with the exception of Mayor Collins who did not vote 
(mayoral appointment).

to determine whether compliance of annexation conditions required by Wyoming Statute §15-1-
402 exist for Saddle Ridge, 18th Filing (located northwest of the intersection of Christensen Road 
and Pershing Boulevard).

to determine whether compliance of annexation conditions required by Wyoming Statute §15-1-
402 exist for a portion of the S1/2 of Section 27, T.13N., R.67W, of the 6th PM, Laramie County,

Council Chambers, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, WY, and via electronic conference meeting 

for a for a bar and grill license application filed with the City Clerk’s Office for Billy Jacks – 
Wyoming LLC d/b/a Billy Jack’s Pizza Pub, 4020 Airport Parkway West Suite 1, Cheyenne, WY. 
 
Announcement of a Public Hearing to be held May 9, 2022 at 6:00 p.m. in City Council 
Chambers, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, WY, and via electronic conference meeting for a for 
a microbrewery license application filed with the City Clerk’s Office for Blue Raven Brewery, 
LLC, d/b/a Blue Raven Brewery, LLC, 209 E. 18th St., Cheyenne, WY. 
 
Under other business:   
 
 
Jim Ridgeway inquired on the process of cleaning storm drains.  Vicki Nemecek, Director 
of Public Works, explained the inlet cleaning process which includes one truck and one crew that 
starts downtown and works their way out to clean about 3,000 inlets once a year.  She noted the 
cleanings can only take place when weather cooperates and if there is a particular problematic 
area, a request can be called in for maintenance.  
 
 
Upon inquiry by Mr. Ridgeway, Mayor Collins provided information on outstanding 
parking tickets and how much was collected during the amnesty period noting outstanding   
parking fees continue to be a challenge that is being addressed.  He further explained that typically 
people who have outstanding tickets are aware they have them and they aren’t paying them.   
 
 
Mr. Laybourn advised he recently visited the mobile home park located at 316 Central 
Avenue with the Building and Nuisance department directors.  He explained the situation and 
condition of the mobile home park is out of control with trailer homes that are busted open and 
debris scattered everywhere, noting the property owner isn’t capable of remedying the issue.  He 
expressed the urgency of cleaning up the property, particularly cleaning up debris along Crow 
Creek.  Mr. Laybourn referenced issues with the lien process, trouble with property in the past and 
also with scenarios that involve the city cleaning up the property and not being able to collect 
money. Mayor Collins advised he and the Chief of Staff recently visited the area and they are 
working with staff to start a process to clean up the area. 
 
 
Dr. Aldrich noted she had attended the Cheyenne Animal Shelter Fur Ball recently and 
provided information on money raised for the event that will help with the facility and taking care 
of animals in the community. 
 
 
Mr. Johnson provided information on 3 upcoming events that benefit Rooted in Cheyenne 
starting Friday, April 29th at Freedom’s Edge, May 7th at Tribe Tattoo and May 14th, which is the 
spring planting day. 
 
 
Mr. Roybal moved to meet in executive session pursuant to Wyoming Statute §16-4-
405(a)(iii)(vii)(x), seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body.  Mayor Collins announced members would meet in executive session, not open 
to the public; requested the City Attorney, City Clerk, City Treasurer, Chief of Staff and members 
of Planning and Development staff be in attendance and stated there would be no action taken by 
the governing body as a result of the executive session, and that the regular City Council meeting 

would officially conclude at the close of the executive session. (Clerk’s Note: executive session 
convened at 7:42 p.m.) 
 
 
 
Following closure of the executive session, Mayor Collins reconvened the regular meeting 
at 8:29 p.m. 
 
 
There being no further business to come before the governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 8:29 p.m. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Submitted by, 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Kristina F. Jones,   
City Clerk 
 
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request. 
 
Published: 
Wyoming Tribune-Eagle 
 
 
May 4, 2022

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
March 28, 2022 
 
 
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. at Storey Gym, 2811 House Ave., and via electronic conference meeting.  Present 
were: MAYOR – Patrick Collins, COUNCIL MEMBERS – Dr. Michelle Aldrich, Bryan Cook, 
Ken Esquibel, Richard Johnson, Dr. Mark Rinne, Scott Roybal, and Tom Segrave.  Absent were: 
Pete Laybourn and Jeff White. Also present: Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk.  The pledge of 
allegiance was recited. 
 
 
Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to 
be routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no 
separate discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support 
by two other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered 
in its normal sequence on the agenda.  Mr. Roybal moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Cook.  
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.  
 
[CA] 
Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on March 14, 2022.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried.

commented on the historic value of the mansion and his excitement in being a part of its continued

ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Amending the Unified Development Code (UDC) to 
create a Common Landscaping Plan and definition of Development Footprint.  Reporting for 
Public Services Committee, Mr. Cook moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by 
Mr. Segrave.  Mr. Cook moved to postpone to April 11, 2022, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  It was 
noted that staff is working on the amendment language which is anticipated to be complete in the 
next 2 weeks.  Motion to postpone carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body 
present.   
 
ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Amending the Unified Development Code (UDC) 
repealing UDC 5.4.7, UU – Urban Use Overlay District, and creating UDC 5.6.9, UU – Urban Use 
Overlay District.  Mr. Cook moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Roybal. 
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present. (#4423) 
 
ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from P Public District to CB Community Business 
for land located southwest of and adjacent to the intersection of Cleveland Avenue and U.S. 30.  
Mr. Cook moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Motion carried. 
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present. (#4424) 
 
ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from MUB Mixed-Use Business Emphasis to CB 
Community Business for land located southeast of the intersection of College Drive and Charles 
Street.  Mr. Cook moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Motion carried. 
Voting “yes”- all members of the governing body present. 
 
ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Creating a new Section 9.01.010, Definitions of Title 9, 
Public Peace and Welfare, of the Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Mr. Cook moved to 
approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. Johnson.  Mr. Johnson moved to postpone to April 
11, 2022, seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Bob Wilson voiced support for the proposed postponement, 
and noted he is working with city staff on language amendments and expressed appreciation to 
Stefanie Boster, City Attorney, and council members Dr. Aldrich and Mr. Johnson for the attention 
to his concerns.  Annette Williams expressed concern regarding the lack of support she feels she 
received at the March 22, 2022, Public Services Committee meeting.  Ms. Williams explained she 
had requested to speak following completion of action taken on the committee agenda and was 
told it was not customary for council committee meetings to receive public comment except for 
comment relating to items on the agenda in proper order and she questioned this practice.  It was 
noted that Public Services Committee Chairman Bryan Cook did allow Ms. Williams to speak as 
a courtesy.  Mayor Collins explained that the governing body allows for public comment on 
general city business at the end of every City Council meeting and that they are not required to 
and have not allowed for public comment periods at standing committees for many years.  Georgia 
Broyles, Youth Alternatives, questioned if there would be a process for School Resource Officers 
to ticket students violating the ordinance.  It was noted the request would be looked into. Sam 

Kayne and Pastor Tom Perea expressed support of the proposed postponement.  Motion to 
postpone carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.

Agreement between the Board of Public Utilities and Polo Ranch Company regarding 
Quarter Circle Five subdivision well development and associated water entitlement.  Hank Bailey 
advised he is on the Polo Ranch Company Board of Directors and has proposed language to satisfy 
legal terms of the agreement.  Mr. Bailey provided information relating to past and proposed water 
usage involving rural properties and future development of residential homes.  Levi Martin, area 
resident, voiced concerns involving the city relinquishing water rights, fairness and previously 
agreed upon policy terms.  Brad Brooks, BOPU Director, upon inquiry provided information 
involving a previous water usage study completed in Laramie County.  Mr. Brooks provided 
information relating to proposed water usage allowances and actual usage on the Polo Ranch and 
requirement of the ranch to pay for any overuse, noting the Polo Ranch water usage is monitored.  
Mr. Brooks reported a previous Supreme Court opinion that determined a use of .7 acre feet per 
minute for county residential properties and advised he feels that is a fair assessment.  It was noted 
that the water use of the proposed residential home development would not be metered.  Comment 
was made that the state allows for up to 25 acre feet per minute for rural wells.  Mr. Brooks further 
noted that the proposed assessment provides a savings to the aquafer over all, noting support of 
the stipulations in this agreement. Information was provided regarding the 1955 agreement.  Mr. 
Johnson moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Mr. Johnson moved to amend by adding 
#10 which states “the Polo Ranch Company hereby acknowledges the binding rulings of the 
Wyoming Supreme Court in their 1998 and 2003 opinions in litigation involving Polo Ranch 
Company and the  City of Cheyenne and hereby expressly agrees that the 1955 Drilling and Water 
Use Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and John and Marie Bell has been finally and fully 
adjudicated and that the Polo Ranch has no claims or causes of action against the City of Cheyenne 
based on that Agreement or its implementation.”, seconded by Dr. Aldrich.  Motion to amend 
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.     Main motion as amended 
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present with the exception of Mr. Cook 
and Dr. Rinne voting “no”.  (#7487)
