Consideration of Bid #E-16-22 for furnishing bituminous asphalt hot mix, tack oil, and 
cold mix on an annual basis for the City of Cheyenne, Street & Alley Division (2019-2022 1% 
Sales Tax). Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (accepted bid from Knife River d/b/a JTL Group, 
Inc. Cheyenne, in an amount not to exceed $434,500.00). 
 
[CA] 
Consideration of Bid #E-17-22 for furnishing aggregates on an annual basis for the City of 
Cheyenne, Street & Alley Division (2019-2022 1% Sales Tax). Motion (per Consent Agenda) 
carried (accepted bid from Croell Inc., Cheyenne, in an amount not to exceed $37,500.00). 
 
[CA] 
Consideration to purchase between the City of Cheyenne and MED-ENG, LLC, for the 
purchase of two (2) bomb suits for the City of Cheyenne, Cheyenne Police Department (U.S. 
Department of Homeland Security Grant).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (in an amount 
not to exceed $71,318.00). 
 
[CA] 
Consideration to purchase between the City of Cheyenne, Cheyenne Fire & Rescue 
Department, and Stryker, Portage, MI, for equipment maintenance services.  (General Fund) 
Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (in an amount not to exceed $59,388.75).   
 
[CA] 
Consideration of Bid #E-8-22 for a contract between the City of Cheyenne and Pine Bluffs 
Gravel & Excavating for Yard Waste Material Hauling and Fill Dirt Hauling for the City of 
Cheyenne, Compost Facility (Solid Waste Fund) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] 
Consideration of Bid #E-19-22 for furnishing 95 Gallon Automated Refuse Carts for the 
City of Cheyenne, Sanitation Division (Solid Waste Fund) - referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] 
Request to approve an additional dispensing area for City of Cheyenne Limited Retail 
Liquor Licensee Cheyenne Country Club, located at 800 Stinner Rd., Cheyenne, Wyoming- 
referred to Finance Committee. 
 

No comments were made on the Voucher Report as presented. 
 
 
No comments were made on the Revenue Report as presented. 
 
 
Appointments for Erin LeBlanc to the Board of Adjustment and Elizabeth Bales to the 
Historic Preservation Board.  Mr. Roybal moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Mayor 
Collins upon inquiry provided information on the application and selection process for mayoral 
appointments to boards and commissions.  Mayor Collins noted that it is possible for individuals 
to serve on multiple community boards at the same time and that the application by Elizabeth Bales 
was not vetted by the Historic Preservation Board prior to his recommendation to appoint Ms. 
Bales to the board.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present 
with the exception of Mayor Collins who did not vote (Mayoral appointment). 
 
 
Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held April 11, 2022 at 6:00 p.m. in 
City Council Chambers, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, WY, and via electronic conference 
meeting, to hear public comment regarding the City’s intent to purchase property located at 1800 
Westland Road, Cheyenne, Wyoming for the purpose of relocating the Cheyenne Transit System 
Office and Transfer Station. 
 
 
Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held April 11, 2022 at 6:00 p.m. in 
City Council Chambers, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, WY, and via electronic conference 
meeting, for one available retail liquor license application filed with the City Clerk’s Office by 
Ace’s Range LLC d/b/a Ace’s Range LLC, 1505 Pioneer Ave., Big D Oil Co. d/b/a Big D Oil #56, 
4100 Whitney Rd., Billy Jacks - Wyoming LLC d/b/a Billy Jack’s Pizza Pub, 4020 Airport 
Parkway West, Suite 1, Capitol Cuisine LLC d/b/a Ike’s Bar & Grill, 122 W. 6th St., Ful-O-Pep 
d/b/a The Old Cheyenne Elevator, 800 W. 21st St., Get Bent, LLC d/b/a Get Bent, LLC, 707 W. 
Lincolnway, Maurice W. Brown d/b/a Town & Country Supermarket Liquors-North, 2120 Carey 
Ave., Space 1, Peaches’ Package Liquor & Bar LLC d/b/a Peaches’ Package Liquor & Bar LLC, 
Anderson Corner, 3rd Filing, Lot 2, Block 1, Vitamin College Natural Food Markets, Inc. d/b/a 
Natural Grocers, 1851 Dell Range Blvd., Westby Edge LLC d/b/a Westby Edge LLC, 714 W. 20th 
St. and Wyoming Downs OTB 14 LLC d/b/a Wyoming Downs OTB 14, 1345 Dell Range Blvd., 
Cheyenne. 
 
 
Under other business:   
 
 
Annette Williams advised that she felt that she did not get the same treatment at the March 
22, 2022 Public Services Committee as other members of the public and felt disrespected.  Ms. 
Williams requested approval to hang military memorial banners in downtown and other areas of 
town during the Veteran’s Day holiday.  She noted that family members of fallen military would 
be paying for the banners and the brackets will be used if approved by the Board of Public Utilities.  
Ms. Williams inquired about amending the current UDC and City Code sign regulations to allow 
for banners to be hung during certain time frames.  Mayor Collins advised that unfortunately by 
allowing one group to hang banners it opens up the possibility that the city would be required to 
allow any group to hang banners.  Mayor Collins advised groups such as “hate groups” that may 
offend others would be allowed to hang their banners and reported that other Wyoming towns have 
been sued relating to this issue.  Mayor Collins reported that he is looking into options to allow 

groups such as the military to be able to be honored in ways suggested by Ms. Williams.  Ms. 
Williams inquired about scooters randomly placed around town and if that was a code violation.  
City Attorney Stefanie Boster advised they are not in violation of right of way ordinances.    
 
 
Jim Ridgeway commented on revisions made to recent bid inquiries involving city surplus 
firearms.  He voiced concerns relating to the city’s ability to sell firearms without serial numbers, 
absence of visual inspection either in person or by photographs and method of current storage by 
the city.    
 
 
Mayor Collins upon inquiry advised the Municipal Building repairs are near completion 
and that there does not appear there were financial savings during staff’s absence from the building 
during the past 6 months.     
 
 
 Bob Wilson voiced support for the postponement on the ordinance relating to creating a 
new Section 9.01.010-Definitions of Title 9 Public Peace and Welfare and looking into code 
changes relating to community signage.    
 
 
There being no further business to come before the governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 7:15 p.m. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Submitted by, 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Kristina F. Jones,   
City Clerk 
 
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request. 
 
Published: 
Wyoming Tribune-Eagle 
 
 
April 13, 2022 
 


--- END OF FILE ---

ACE’S RANGE LLC
1505 Pioneer Ave (307) 631-7181
acesrangewygmail.com
The Honorable Patrick Collins and Members of the Cheyenne City Council
Municipal Building
Mayor’s Office Room 310
2101 O’Neil Avenue
Cheyenne, WY 82001
RE: Application for Retail Liquor License
Honorable Mayor Collins and Members of the Cheyenne City Council,
Ace’s Range is a new virtual simulation where popular outdoor activities are done inside with
virtual simulators. In our case, we propose utilizing both golfing and shooting state of the art
simulators. A similar venue, XGolf, has been open in Fort Collins since 2019 and brings in
visitors, sales tax and property tax. Equally important Ace’s Range fills the empty warehouse
space at 1
Street and Pioneer Avenue with another quality family and adult venue in
downtown Cheyenne.
We respectfully request your consideration in granting our business the newly available retail
liquor license. It is imperative to this type of business that we have the ability to serve alcohol to
the extent only allowed with a full retail license.
We know you will be considering a lot of requests and need as much information as possible to
make an informed decision. The most salient topics we would like to briefly touch on in this
letter are as follows:
15th Street Renaissance: We share the Governing Body’s passion for revitalizing downtown and
believe in the importance of the 15th Street Renaissance to that mission. Ace’s Range will be
located at the corner of I 5th Street and Pioneer in a 1940’s warehouse that is vacant. We will
completely rehabilitate the interior and exterior of the building and create a vibrant property at
this important corner. We intend to partner with local businesses in the vicinity as well as local
organizations in the city to maximize the use of our simulators and facility.
Economic Activity: Partnerships with downtown businesses, property tax, sales tax,
partnerships with golf organizations and shooting clubs. Ace’s Range will offer leagues and
special events to attract business to the downtown area. Ace’s Range, in accordance with
Laramie County Health regulations, would like to offer food from downtown restaurants to be
delivered by Ace’s Range. Ace’s Range would also work with the food trucks to create a

schedule to add even more variety to our food options. In addition to these options we will
provide some basic food offerings that do not require a commercial kitchen.
Business Plan: If granted this license, we have two phases to our business plan. Phase I is to
complete the interior and exterior rehabilitation of the building. We have assembled the
professionals necessary to begin work on the building in compliance with regulations and code.
Currently Phase I is estimated to be completed by early May.
Phase II is expected to be completed by September 1, 2022. Phase II is when we bring in the
simulator equipment and open our doors to our customers! Our building is 7,500 square feet and
all age groups will be welcome. We are a family oriented business with a designated serving area
that only comprises approximately 320 square feet out of the 7,500. Signage and pony walls will
clearly designate our serving area. North of our serving area we will have a space for minors to
order non-alcoholic beverages and refreshments.
Ace’s Range will maintain a “seasonal area” comprising almost 1,000 square feet in which we
will do promotional events. For example, during CFD we may possibly offer the space to a
western boutique vendor, or set up a stage for live music. Ace’s Range will have large screens
for sports television. For the big game, the space could be filled with more seating and tables or
it could be reserved for private parties. Our mission of being a family oriented business will be
demonstrated in our flexibility of that space. Ace’s Range plans to run a fun and engaging
promotional campaign using our point of sale system Tee Snap.
Simulator Technology: Ace’s Range is truly a new and unique experience for Cheyenne. We
will feature six Trackman golf bays and three Smokeless Range shooting simulator bays. Two of
the six Trackman bays will be in a privately enclosed area with their own ambiance separate
from the rest of the building. Trackman is a state-of-the-art golf simulator featuring premiere
courses from all over the world. Trackman is the best medium to teach the game of golf via the
numbers provided for every swing. Smokeless Range is a software that utilizes a laser bullet to
simulate discharging a firearm over various applications. These applications include fast draw,
hostage scenarios, zombies, moving targets and of course standard shooting ranges. Ace’s
Range will host leagues for golf and shooting.
Competition: Lastly, with any license, we know you have to consider the potential for
competition with existing businesses and licensees. There are two other known facilities that
have a golf simulator in town. The unique nature of our facility serves a different demand in the
community than the other two businesses. We are not aware of anyone doing virtual shooting
ranges.
We look forward to discussing our project more with you in the coming weeks.
Sincerely,
J Breht “JB” William Kunz
General Manager
Ace’s Range LLC

Dear Mayor and City Council,
Big D Oil believes in growing with the community and offer
service the community has requested.
We wish to be considered for a liquor license for our new
location at 4100 Whitney Rd, Cheyenne WY 82001. There we
will sell retail package liquor only. Our cashiers will be tips
trained and our passport registers will require ID scan for age
verification.
We appreciate your consideration in this matter.
Thank You,
Big D Oil Co.

To Whom It May Concern
Big D Oil will be in compliance at all times regarding the
sate of liquor. The cashiers and manager will be Tips
trained before being allowed to ring up any alcohol sales.
We will have the standard signage stating no one under
21 will be sold alcohol and the customer must have a
valid ID to scan.

Business Plan for Big D Oil Store 4*56
Big D Oil is an established Corporation with several stores
in operation. Big D employs 60+ employees in Cheyenne.
At the location of 4100 Whitney Road we are there to
meet the needs of the community by providing:
*Convenient location for a growing area to serve the
community.
* The liquor store along with our other offerings such as,
fueling services, restrooms, hot food, sporting goods,
groceries and general merchandise will generate tax
income for the City of Cheyenne.
* Cashiers for liquor store are to be Tips trained to
deliver responsible liquor sales.
* Big D operates several liquor stores in the state of
Wyoming successfully and responsibly.
*We offer competitive pricing that customers appreciate
and creates return business which creates higher
revenue.
*We offer fair and competitive wages for employment
opportunities as we will be staffing this store with an
additional 10-15 employees.
*pay for cashiers ranges $11 to $13 an hour.

F.
* Assistant Managers average is $15 an hour.
* Managers range averages between $50,000 to $60,000
at this time.
*Hours of operation of the store will be 6am to 10pm.
We look forward to serving the community we live and
work in.
Financial Plan
Our newly built store on the corner of Highway 30 and
Whitney Rd will be completed in March and is solely
owned by Big D Oil. Big D has invested millions of dollars
in the building at this location. Big D has been in
business over 50 years and have met all financial
obligations for this project. We have designed the store
in such a manner that the liquor store would meet all
requirements.
Thank you for your consideration.
BigDOil
/

CHEYENNE REGIONAL AIRPORT- LEASE NO. BILLJ-OO1
THIS LEASE, made and entered into this 1st day September 2021 by and between the
CHEYENNE REGIONAL AIRPORT BOARD, hereinafter referred to as the “Board,” party of the
firstpart, and BILLY JACK’S WYOMING LLC, a company organized under the laws of the State of
Wyoming, hereinafter referred to as “Lessee,” party of the second part;
W1TNESS ETH:
WHEREAS, the Cheyenne Regional Airport is owned, operated, and maintained by the Cheyenne
Regional Airport Board, located within the City of Cheyenne in the State of Wyoming; and
WHEREAS, the Lessee desires to lease certain premises upon the Airport more fully
described in paragraph one below; and
WHEREAS, the Board is willing to lease same to Lessee under the terms
and conditions hereinafter set forth;
NOW, THEREFORE, for and in consideration of the premises and of the mutual covenants
and agreements herein contained, the parties hereto agree as follows:
1.
LEASED AREA: The Board hereby leases and demises unto the Lessee, and
the Lessee herebyhires and takes from the Board, the following described premises, all at the
Cheyenne Regional Airport, Cheyenne, Wyoming in the Airport Terminal Building, as
shown in red on the enclosedExhibit “A” incorporated herein by this reference:
A. 2000 square feet, more or less, of restaurant and food service prep/storage space.
B. Lessee will have access to the common areas of the terminal corridor for
additional seatiiig, special events, etc., with prior permission from the Airport
Director.
C. Lessee agrees and understands the restrooms facilities located in the terminal arenon
exclusive.
1

2.
TENANT’S ACCEPTANCE OF PROPERTY:
Neither the Board nor its agents have
made any representations with respect to the leased property except as expressly set forth
herein and no rights, casements, or licenses are acquired by the Lessee by implication or
otherwise except as expressly set forth in the provisions of this lease. The taking of
possession of the leased property by the Lessee shall be conclusive evidence that the Lessee
accepts the same “as is
and that the leased property was in good condition at the time
possession was taken.
3.
TERM: The initial term of this lease shall be five (5) years and shall commence as of 12:01
A,M., Mountain Daylight Time, on the 1st day of September 2021 and end at 11:59 P.M.,
Mountain Daylight Time, on the 3 1’ day of August 2025. Lessee has the option to execute
a two-year (2) extension to this lease under the same terms by providing the Board notice
ninety (90-days) in advance of the original termination date of the lease.
4.
FEES: The Lessee covenants and agrees to pay and the Board hereby reserves as rent based
on the following schedule.
A. 1,920 sq/ft @ $7.00 = $13,440.00 Annually or $1,120.00 monthly.
B. Five Thousand ($5,000) refundable security deposit.
The above outlined fees shall be paid in monthly installments at the office of the Director of
Aviation, each such installment to be due and payable in advance on or before the 1st day
of each calendar month of the term hereof.
In the event any fees required hereunder are not paid within fifteen (15) days of the due date,
such sums shall be assessed a late charge at the rate of three percent (3%) per month until
paid. Such charges shall be due and owing whether Lessor elects to terminate the lease or
waive the default.
These fees will be waived for the first tweLve (L2) calendar months of the lease. Subsequent
fees will be assessed after one-year of restaurant operation, and upon a mutual agreement of
2

both parties following a review of the first year of operation. The Fees outlined in this lease
are subject to change after year-one.
5.
ALTERATIONS: Any remodeling or alterations must have the prior written approval of
the Board. Any remodeling or alterations which take place without the Board’s prior written
approval shall be removed and the premises restored to its original condition, at the Board’s
request, and at the Lessee’s sole expense. Title to all improvements constructed or instalLed
by the Lessee in or on the demised premises (except trade fixtures which can be removed
without damage to the property), and all materials forming part thereof, shall pass to the
Board as the same or any part thereof are constructed or installed or otherwise affixed to
said premises.
6.
USE OF PREMISES: The Lessee shall have the right and privilege to occupy the demised
premises for the purpose of operating a restaurant/beverage concession and similar related
businesses for the preparation and retail sale to the public of food and beverages, including
satisfactory provision for ingress and egress to and from said premises.
Alcohol or Malt
Beverage May be sold on Premises. Lessee may engage, upon the demised premises, only
in those activities which are enumerated herein and no other, without the prior written
permission of the Board, and Lessee shall use the demised premises for no other or unlawful
purposes whatsoever.
Lessee shall not commit or permit any nuisance from or upon the
leased premises.
The Lessee shall, at all times during the term hereof, actively use the leased areas for the
purpose herein specified and shall not at any time Leave any part of the leased area vacant
without the Airport Board’s prior written consent. In the event Lessee abandons any part of
the leased area for a period of thirty (30) days, such part may be reclaimed by the Director
of Aviation, unilaterally deLeted from this lease, and devoted to another use.
All the Lessee’s personnel shall be neat and clean in appearance and be appropriately
licensed. Lessee’s personnel shall comply with all food handling requirements set forth by
3

state and local agencies.
Lessees personnel shall have automobile parking privileges in
areas designated by the Director of Aviation.
In addition to the area listed in Paragraph I, Lessee shall have the non-exclusive right to
provide food and beverage service, in accordance with State, Local and Transportation
Security Administration regulations, in the passenger holding area located behind the main
terminal on the Aircraft Operations Area (AOA).
7.
OPERATIONS
OF
BUSINESS:
Lessee
shall
actively
operate
a
full-service
restaurant/beverage concession using the entire leased area and shall use its best effort to
accomplish a business-like operation. The food service facility shall, at a minimum be
available to meet the reasonable demand for both airline passengers and members of the
general public. The Board hereby grants the Director of Aviation the authority to consult
directly with Lessee, if required, to establish hours of operation in the best interest of the
Airport. Minimum requirements require service for all regularly scheduled airline flights.
Lessee may close the facility on selected days or holidays with prior permission from the
Director of Aviation.
In no event shall the hours of operation be curtailed to an extent that
the service contemplated under this Lease shall be diminished.
8.
OUALITY AND CHARACTER OF SERVICE: It is expressly agreed and understood
that all food offered by Lessee shall be of a quality and quantity which is comparable to, or
better than, that offered for sale in similar establishments in the Cheyenne area and the
service shall be prompt, clean, courteous, and efficient.
In the operation of the business, Lessee shall establish and charge, or shall cause to be
established and charged, such rates and charges for the service, commodities and
merchandise furnished as, in each case, shall be competitive to those charged for similar
services, commodities and merchandise in comparable establishments in Cheyenne with due
regard, however, for difference in quality of merchandise or services offered. A schedule of

the rates and charges established shall be subject to review by Lessor prior to their effective
date.
The Lessor shall have the right to inspect the conditions of the premises, the quality and
quantity of the food, drink, the articles sold, the character of the service, the appearance and
performance of service, personnel, and to work together to remedy any questionable
practices.
Lessee shall at all times during regular business hours maintain on the Airport, or via a
reliable contact phone number, a manager or repreSentative authorized to represent and act
for Lessee in matters pertaining to the operation of the Lease.
9.
UTILITIES:
Lessor shall be responsible for costs to provide area with heat and air
conditioning, water, sewer, and waste removal charges included in the leased area. Lessee
agrees to pay one hundred (100%) of the total utility for gas and electric for the entire
restaurant space. Lessee shall be responsible for all costs related to phone services, cable
services, and cleaning of leased premises.
10. MAINTENANCE:
Board shall keep and maintain the roof and structural portions of the
leased premises, the plumbing and sewage facilities, heating, air conditioning and
mechanical equipment, if equipment owned by Lessor, except that only damage thereto
caused by an act or negligence of Lessee, its employees, agents, invitees, subtenants,
licensees, assignees, or contractors, in which event such damage shall be promptly reported
by Lessee. Other than as herein provided, Board shall not be responsible to maintain or make
any improvements or repairs of any kind in or upon the leased premises.
If cleaning of the grease trap or servicing/cleaning of the vent hoods becomes a requirement
as a direct result of Lessee’s operation, such activity shall be the responsibility of the Lessee.
11. ASSIGNMENT AND SUBLETTING: This lease is not available for assignment of
subletting.
5

12. AGREEMENTS WITH THE UNITED STATES OR THE STATE OF WYOMiNG:
This lease is subject and subordinate to the terms, reservations, restrictions, provisions and
conditions of any existing or future agreement between the Cheyenne Regional Airport and
the United States or the State of Wyoming relative to the operation and maintenance of the
Cheyenne Regional Airport and its appurtenant facilities, the execution of which has been
or may be required as a condition precedent to the participation by any federal or state agency
in the extension, expansion or development of said airport or its facilities.
13. MINORiTY BUSINESS ENTERPRISES: Lessee hereby assures that no person shall be
excluded from participation in, denied the benefits of, or otherwise discriminated against, in
connection with the award and performance of any contract including leases covered by 49
Code Federal Regulations, Part 23, on the grounds of race, color, national origin or sex.
Lessee further assures that it will include the language of this paragraph in all sub-leases and
cause all sub-leases to similarly include this clause in further sub-leases.
14. COMPLIANCE WITH LAWS: The Lessee covenants that it will use the demised premises
in accordance with all applicable laws and ordinances as well as in compliance with
regulations either governing or adopted by the Board for the management, control, and
operation of the Cheyenne Regional Airport.
15. SIGNS: Lessee shall not paint or maintain any signs whatsoever, upon the demised premises
without the prior written permission of the Director of Aviation.
16. RIGHT OF INSPECTION:
The Board reserves and retains for its officers, employees,
and authorized representatives the full and unrestricted right to enter the premises for an
emergency for the purpose of inspecting or protecting such premises and of doing any and
all things which the Board deems necessary for the proper general conduct and operation of
the Cheyenne Regional Airport. For non-emergency inspections the Board will provide a
24-hour notice of such inspection.
6

17,
CANCELLATION AND TEJMINATlON:
The Board may cancel and terminate this
lease without cause, and may repossess the premises, with or without process of law, without
liability, in the event any installment of rent, or other payment provided for herein, is in
arrears, and remains unpaid for fifteen (15) days after the same is due.
In the event of a
default as to any other provision of this lease, the board shall give no less than thirty (30)
days written notice to Lessee of its intention to so terminate, at the end of which time alL the
rights of Lessee hereunder shall terminate unless such default, which shall have been stated
in such notice, shall have been cured within such thirty (30) days; provided, however, the
Lessee shall be allowed only two such notices in any 12 month period which it may cure
within the time specified in this paragraph. The third such notice in any 12-month period
shall be final and shall cancel and terminate all of the rights hereunder of the Lessee, without
any right on the part of the Lessee to cure default after receiving such notice.
Further, in the event the Lessee shall engage in any activity or practice (except as herein
specifically permitted) which hinders or interferes with the proper use and operation of the
Airport, then the Airport may order the Lessee to forthwith cease and desist from such
activity or practice, and should the Lessee fail or refuse to comply with any such order within
thirty (30) days, then the Airport may, at its option, cancel and terminate this lease.
In the event of cancellation and termination of this lease by the Board as herein provided,
the Lessee shall be liable to the Board for the balance of the rent herein reserved and all costs
of collection, including reasonable attorneys fees.
In event of the death of Lessee the lease will automaticaLly terminate at the end of the month
in which death occurs. In the event of a disability of Lessee rendering Lessee unable to
perform under this lease, lease shall be terminable by Lessee upon thirty (30) days written
notice to the Board. The Lessee may negotiate with the Lessor for the suspension or
reduction of the payment of fees called for under this Agreement during any period that the
7

Cheyenne Regional Airport is not operated by the Lessor for use by certified scheduled air
carriers.
18. TAXES. LICENSES. DEBTS: The Lessee covenants and agrees to pay promptly all valid
taxes, excises, license fees, permit fees, bills, debts and obligations incurred by it in
connection with its operation of said business on the leased premises, and not permit the
same to become delinquent and to suffer no lien, mortgage, judgment or execution to be filed
against said property or improvements thereon which will in any way be an impairment to
the right of the Board under this lease.
19, INSURANCE: At all times during the term of this lease, and of any renewal or extension
hereof, Lessee agrees that it will, at its own cost and expense, provide and keep in force for
the benefit of the Lessee and the Board as additional insured, a policy or policies of insurance
as follows:
A.
Bodily injury and property damage combined single limit liability insurance in an
amount not less than $1,000,000.
B.
Property damage on Lessee personal property in an amount sufficient to replace all
fixtures.
Certificates evidencing such insurance as current and active shall be kept on file in the
Airport Administration office at all times during the term of the lease.
20. INDEMNIFICATION OF BOARD AGAINST CLAIMS OF PERSONAL INJURY
OR PROPERTY DAMAGE: Lessee shall defend, indemnify and hold the Board harmless
from and against any claim, loss, expense or damage to any person or property in or upon
the leased premises or any area allocated to or used exclusively by Lessee or its agents,
employees or invitees, arising out of Lesse&s use or occupancy of said premises, or any act
or neglect of Lessee or Lessees servants, employees or agents, or any change, alteration or
improvement made by Lessee in the leased premises.
K

21. WAIVERS: No waiver or default by the Board of any of the terms, covenants, or conditions
to he performed, kept, and observed by the Lessee shall be construed as, or operate as, a
waiver by the Board of any subsequent default of any of the terms, covenants or conditions
herein contained to be performed, kept, and observed by the Lessee.
22. SURRENDER AND HOLDING OVER: The Lessee covenants that at the expiration of
the period for which the demised premises are leased to it, or at the earlier termination of
the letting thereof, it will quit, and surrender said premises in good state and condition,
reasonable wear and tear excepted. Should the Lessee hold over the use of or continue to
occupy the demised premises after expiration of the lease, such holding over will be as a
tenant at sufferance for which use it shall pay rent at double the amount herein provided and,
in addition, be liable for any and all damages resulting from such tenancy.
23.
OUIET ENJOYMENT:
The Board covenants for itself, its successors and assigns that
Lessee, upon payment of the rent and performance of Lessee’s other covenants herein, shall
and may peaceably and quietly have, hold, and enjoy the demised premises during the term
of this lease.
24.
NOTICES:
Any notices required or given hereunder shall be given by first class mail,
telegram or personal delivery addressed to the respective addresses below, or such other
address as may be specified in writing from time to time:
BOARD
LESSEE
Cheyenne Regional Airport Board
Billy Jack’s Pizza
— Wyoming LLC
Peter S. Illoway, President
Bill J. Winberg, President
P.O. Box 2210
P.O. Box 818
Cheyenne, WY 82003
Kearney, NE 68848
25. SECURITY: in addition to Lessee’s responsibilities to maintain the premises as provided
herein, it shall particularly maintain the premises in such a manner as to keep them secure
from unauthorized intrusion and shall, with respect to any area of the premises opening to
the air operations area of the airport, provide for an adequate security system designed to
9

prevent unauthorized persons or vehicles from
entering such air operations area.
An “air
operations area
means any area of the airport used or intended to be used for landing,
takeoff, or surface maneuvering of aircraft. An ‘adequate security system’ means providing
for security at a standard no less than required and set out in Part 1542, Transportation
Security Regulations., as amended.
At any time during the term hereof, when Lessee is
requested in writing by the Board, the Lessee shall submit to the Board the security plans
that are to be used and are being used by the Lessee on any or all of the premises, and the
Director of Aviation reserves the right in his absolute discretion to require any different or
further security provisions to be implemented and maintained by the Lessee.
26. TERMINATION
OF
LEASE
UPON
CONDEMNATION
-
RENT
APPORTIONMENT ENTIRE AWARD TO BOARD:
If the leased property, or any
party thereof is taken by eminent domain or sold under the threat of the exercise of said
power (all of which are herein called
condemnation”), this lease shall expire on the date
when the leased property shall be so taken, and the rent shall be apportioned as of that date.
No part of any award shall belong to the Lessee.
27. RESTORATION
BY
BOARD OF PROPERTY DESTROYED
BY
INSURED
CASUALTY TENANT TO REPLACE TRADE FIXTURES
-- PROPORTIONATE
RENT ABATEMENT BOARD’S OPTION TO TERMINATE: If the leased premises
or the building containing it should be damaged or destroyed during the lease term by fire
or other insurable casualty without the fault of the Lessee, the Board shall, subject to the
time that elapses due to adjustment of fire insurance, repair and)or restore the same to
substantially the condition it was in immediately prior to such damage or destruction, except
as in this Article provided. The Board’s obligation under this Article shall in no event exceed
the scope of the work required to be done by the Board in the original construction of the
building. The Board shall not be required to, but Lessee shall, with due dispatch, replace or
restore forthwith any trade fixtures, signs or other installations theretofore installed by
10

Lessee. Rent payable under this lease, shall be abated proportionately according to the floor
area of the leased premises which is usable by the Lessee, but if such damage was caused
by the fault of the Lessee, there shall be no abatement of rent. Such abatement shall continue
for the period commencing with such damage or destruction and ending with the completion
by the Board of such work of repair and/or reconstruction as the Board is obligated to do.
If, however, the leased premises or the building containing it should be damaged or
destroyed by any cause so that the Board shall decide to demolish, abandon or to completely
rebuild the leased premises or the building containing it, the Board may, within 60 days after
such damage or destruction, give Lessee written notice of such decision and thereupon this
lease shall be deemed to have terminated as of the date of the damage or destruction and
Lessee shall immediately quit and surrender the leased premises to the Board.
29. OTHER AGREEMENTS:
A. Declaration of Governing Law.
This lease shall be governed by, construed, and
enforced in accordance with the laws of the State of Wyoming.
B. Plural, Singular, Gender to be Read in Context. Any word contained in the text of
this lease shall be read as the singular or the plural and as the masculine, feminine or
neuter gender as may be applicable in the particular context.
C. Joint and Several Obligation of Lessee, Partners.
If there is more than one party
tenant, the covenants of the Lessee shall be the joint and several obligations of each such
party, and, if the Lessee is a partnership, the covenants of the Lessee shall be the joint
and several obligations of each of the partners and the obligations of the firm.
D. Binding Effect on Parties, Representatives, Successors and Assigns. The provisions
of this lease shall be binding on and inure to the benefit of the parties, their legal
representatives, successors and permitted assigns.
E. Parties have relationship of landlord and Tenant-Not Principal-Agent, Partners or
Joint Venturers. Nothing contained in this lease shall be deemed or construed to create
II

the relationship of principal and agent or of partnership or of joint venture or of any
association whatsoever between the Board and the Lessee, it being expressly understood
and agreed that neither the computation of rent nor any other provisions contained in this
lease nor any act or acts of the parties hereto shall be deemed to create any relationship
between the Board and the Lessee other than the relationship of landlord and tenant.
F. Captions for Convenience-Not part of Lease-Not to Limit or Amplify Lease. The
captions of this lease are for convenience only, are not a part of the lease, and do not in
any way limit or amplify the terms and provisions hereof.
G. Identification of Leased Premises by Annexed Plan. The plan attached to and made
a part of this lease, except as otherwise specifically provided, is used solely for the
purpose of identifying or designating the leased premises. Any marking, measurements,
dimensions or notes of any kind contained therein have no bearing with respect to the
terms and conditions of this lease and are not to be considered a part hereof.
H. Prior Representations Incorporated into Lease-No Representations Warranties or
Promises by Landlord, Agents or Representatives. All negotiations, considerations,
representations, and understandings between the parties are incorporated in this lease,
and Lessee acknowledges that the Board, its agents, and representatives, have made no
representations, warranties, or promises with respect to the leased premises except as
may be expressly set forth herein.
1.
Entire Areement-Modjficatjon in Writing, Signed and Delivered by Both Parties.
This lease contains all of the agreements and representations between the parties. None
of the terms of this lease shall be modified to any extent, except by a written instrument
signed and delivered by both parties.
J.
Severabihh of Invalid or Unenforceable Clause-Remainder of Lease Enforceable-
Clause Applicable to Other Persons and Circumstances.
If any clause, phrase,
provision or portion of this lease or the application thereof to any person or circumstance
12

shall be invalid, or unenforceable under applicable law, such event shall not affect,
impair, or render invalid or unenforceable the remainder of this lease, nor any other
clause, phrase, provision, or portion hereof, nor shall it affect the application of any
clause, phrase, provision, or portion hereof to other persons or circumstances.
30. Non-waiver of Governmental Immunity. Owner expressly reserves its right to invoke
governmental immunity for any claim in tort or contract arising out of this Agreement
pursuant to the Wyoming Governmental Claims Act, Wyo. Stat. 1-39-101 Ct seq. Nothing
herein shall constitute a waiver of any immunity in part or in whole by Owner.
31. Mariano Doctrine. Lessee is hereby on notice of the Mariano doctrine as articulated by the
Wyoming Supreme Court in the case of Mariano & Associates, P.C. v Board of County
Commissioners of Sublette County 737 P.2d 323, Wyo., 1987 and its progeny. The Town
acting through its governing body may not be empowered to bind any future governing body
to the terms of this agreement and specifically reserves all rights granted to future governing
bodies to terminate this agreement pursuant to law.
IN VITNESS WHEREOF, the parties hereto have caused this instrument to be executed as of
the day andyear first above written.

January 26, 2022
Mayor Patrick Collins and Members of City Council
21010’Neil Avenue
Cheyenne, WY 82001
Dear Mayor Collins and Members of City Council;
I am writing on behalf of Billy Jack’s Pizza Pub regarding our company’s entry into a new market
in Cheyenne, Wyoming and to introduce our company to you. Our company redefines fast
casual dining backed by top-notch customer service. We are currently operating in markets
located in Nebraska and exploring locations in Wyoming and Colorado. We are excited to be
expanding our reach to the Cheyenne area.
Our first location in Cheyenne will be located at the Cheyenne Regional Airport. We are excited
to be associated with this essential transportation hub which will contribute to many sectors of
the state and city’s economy and add to the quality of life for Cheyenne residents, businesses
and visitors.
The benefit of our fast casual dining restaurant being located in the Cheyenne Regional Airport
terminal will help increase the direct economic return to the city of Cheyenne and the greater
Laramie County region as our target customers will be those traveling to/from Cheyenne and
those who call Cheyenne home.
Billy Jack’s Pizza Pub and our fifty local Cheyenne investors look forward to bringing a feeling of
a hometown gathering place to the growth at the Cheyenne Regional Airport. We also look
forward to working with our community partners to benefit all citizens in the Cheyenne
community.
Please feel free to contact me at your earliest to discuss any questions you may have.
Thank you, and
I look forward to hearing from you.
Kindest regards,
Bill Win berg, Owner/CEO
Billy Jack’s Pizza Pub

Billy Jack’s - Wyoming, LLC

Page 4 of 4

Alcohol Training for Billy Jack’s
We will be doing online training through Wyoming State Liquor
Association. All employee’s will be TIPS trained and certified through
online training.

Billy Jack’s Wyoming LLC
Business Plan
—
Billy Jack’s will be in the Cheyenne Regional Airport Terminal. It will be the
anchor business in the terminal. We will have operating hours from 7am till 10 pm
to start with. We will adjust these hours based on demand and flight schedules as
the airport grows in number of flights in and out of Cheyenne. We will have a
menu consisting of Pizza, Salads, Subs, and some appetizers for guest to choose
from. We also have a few kids’ friendly items. Our target market is Families and
individuals in Cheyenne along with people flying in and out of the airport. We
want to become a fun gathering place for the community of Cheyenne. We will
employee approximately 30 employees at the airport location with a starting
wage of $9.00 hr plus tip pool. This should net our employees an hourly wages of
approximately $16.00 an hour. We currently have 1 location in Kearney NE and
are opening two locations here in Cheyenne. The second location in Cheyenne will
be a delivery hub only at this time located on S. Greeley HWY. We are looking in
long term to franchise Billy Jack’s to other communities. We have the backing of
50 local investors here in the Cheyenne area. We have a target date of opening in
early April of 2022 or sooner if possible.

COMMERCIAL LEASE
This lease is made between .JKB Inc. dba WyCO Wind and Solar Inc. herein called
Lessor and Capitol Cuisine LLC dba Ike’s Bar & Grill, a Wyoming Corporation herein
called Lessee.
Lessee hereby offers to lease from Lessor the premises described as LOTS; 12, 13, 14 &
15, BLOCK 627 (SOUTH CHEYENNE) also known as 122 W 6th Street, Cheyenne, WY
82007 upon the following TERMS and CONDITIONS.
1. Term and Rent.
Lessor hereby leases the above premises to the Lessee for a term of 24 months,
commencing on September 1, 2020 and terminating on August 30, 2022.
Lessee shall pay Lessor the sum of $2,000.00 (Two Thousand Dollars) per
Month beginning October 1, 2020. Said rental sum shall not be due and payable however
until such time as Lessee opens but no later than July 1,2021. Said rent shall be due and
payable by the 1st of every month after that. Beginning September 1, 2021, said rent shall
increase to $2,400.00 for the remaining 12 month term. All rental payments shall be
made to Lessor, at the address of: AT14N: Donna Marion, P0 Box 3023, Cheyenne,
Wyoming 82003. (307) 631-6855.
2. Use. Lessee shall use and occupy the premise for the purpose of Operating a Bar and
Restaurant and for the sale of alcoholic/malt beverages on premise and for no other
purpose.
3. Care and Maintenance of Premises. Lessee acknowledges that the premises are in good
order and repair, unless otherwise indicated herein. Lessee shall, at his own expense and
at all times, maintain the premises in good and safe condition, including plate glass, and
shall surrender the same at termination hereof, in as good condition as received, normal
wear and tear excepted. Lessee shall be responsible for all repairs required, excepting the
roof, heat/air system, hot water heater, exterior walls and structural foundations.
4. Alterations. Lessee shall not, without first obtaining the written consent ofLessor,
make any alterations, additions, or improvements, in, to or about the premises.
5. Ordinances and Statutes. Lessee shall comply with all statutes, ordinances and
requirements of all municipal, state and federal authorities now in force, or which may
hereafter be in force, pertaining to the premises, occasioned by or affecting the use
thereof by Lessee.
6. Assiginnent and Subletting. Lessee shall not assign this lease.
7. Utilities. All applications and connections for necessary utility services on the demised
premises shall be made in the name ofLessee only, and Lessee shall be solely liable for

which the demised premises may be situated is destroyed to an extent of not less than
one-third ofthe replacement cost, Lessor may elect to terminate this lease whether the
demised premises be injured or not. A total destruction of the building in which the
premises may be situated shall terminate this lease.
14. Lessor’s Remedies on Default. If Lessee defaults in the payment of rent, or any
additional rent, or defaults in the performance of any ofthe other covenants or conditions
hereof, Lessor may give Lessee notice of such default and ifLessee does not cure any
such default within 10 days, after the giving of such notice (or if such other default is of
such nature that it cannot be completely cured within such period, if Lessee does not
commence such curing within 10 days and thereafter proceed with reasonable
diligence and in good faith to cure such default), then Lessor may terminate this lease on
not less than 10 dayst notice to Lessee. On the date specified in such notice the term of
this lease shall terminate, and Lessee shall then quit and surrender the premises to Lessor,
but Lessee shall remain liable as hereinafter provided, ifthis lease shall have been so
terminated by Lessor, Lessor may at any time thereafter resume possession of the
premises by any lawful means and remove Lessee or other occupants and their effects.
No failure to enforce any term shall be deemed a waiver.
15. Security Deposit. Lessee shall deposit with Lessor on the signing ofthis lease the sum
of (None) ($0.00) as security deposit for the performance of Lessee’s obligations under
this lease, including without limitation the surrender ofpossession ofthe premises to
Lessor as herein provided.
16. Attorney’s Fees. In case suit should be brought for recovery ofthe premises, or for
any sum due hereunder, or because of any act which may arise out ofthe possession of
the premises, by either party, the prevailing party shall be entitled to all costs incurred in
connection with such action, including reasonable attorney’s fee.
17. Notices. Any notice which either party may, or is required to give, shall be given
mailing same, postage prepaid, to
Lessor:
1KB Inc. dba WyCO Wind and Solar Inc., 115 W 6th Street, Cheyenne,
Wyoming 82007. 307-286-7437.
Lessee:
Capitol Cuisine dba Ike’s Bar & Grill, 122 W 6th Street, Cheyenne,
Wyoming 82007. 307-760-9381.
18. Heirs, Assigns, Successors. This lease is binding upon and inures to the benefit ofthe
heirs, successors in interest to the parties.
19. No failure ofthe Lessor to enforce any provision herein stated shall be deemed a

2101 O’NeiI Ave
Cheyenne, WY 82001
Esteemed members of Cheyenne City Council and Mayor Collins,
My name is Brian Eicholtz.
I represent Capitol Cuisine LLC and am pleased to come
before you with a proposal of my application for a Retail License to be used at Ike’s Bar & Grill
at 122 w 6 St. Cheyenne WY 82007. My intent is to provide alcoholic beverages with amazing
food in a safe and entertaining environment. Adding a sorely needed establishment to the
South Side of Cheyenne for residents to enjoy a late night drink at a friendly and safe
environment.
I was born and raised in Cheyenne WY on a glorious August evening in 1978. Graduated
from Cheyenne Central in 1997. (Don’t hold that against me Mr. Mayor). Lettering in Basketball
and Soccer. Sticking to my home roots attended the University of Wyoming. After graduation
I returned with my family and was the Bar Manager of Jacksons All American Sports Bar & Grill
here in Cheyenne. 1 year later I was given the promotion of Area Coach to South Eastern
Wyoming Pizza Hut. After 7 years on the job, I decided
I had had enough of the cooperate life
and decided on a personal venture of Capitol Cuisine located at 1720 Capitol Ave here in
Cheyenne WY. After 3 years of supporting and loving downtown Cheyenne I was graced the
opportunity to renovate and create my true passion. Ike’s Bar & Grill was born. Since October
of 2020 I have invested countless hours and finances to create my dream. New flooring,
bathrooms, paint and countless other modifications. We have also been approved to add an
outdoor dining patio.
I look forward to many more years as both a resident of Cheyenne and a
proud member of Downtown Cheyenne.
I am the proud parent of 2 children and 6 stepchildren. With our split family household,
we see All parts of Cheyenne.
My wife Kaylan is not only the glue of our family but finds time
to help at the restaurant and keep up with her career as the Events and Marketing Director of
the Cheyenne Depot Museum. Kaylan is also a graduate of both Cheyenne Central and the
University of Wyoming. Poke pride runs deep in our household.
I appreciate everyone’s time as you entertain my passion.
I cannot imagine a more
thankless job you all seem to do with ease.
I know how extremely busy every single one of you
are. Thank you for all you do for our city and community.
